# Graphics Implementation Task Plan

> Goal: XNA 4.0 Graphics namespace fully implemented — all 4 backends
> (SDL_Renderer, EasyGL, Vulkan, Bgfx).
>
> **SDL_Renderer is intentionally 2D-only.** All 3D calls throw `std::runtime_error`.
> This is correct XNA/FNA behavior — SDL_Renderer cannot do 3D.

> **Task numbering:** 1–663 = original core plan (Phases 1–55, all non-WebGPU work).
> 664+ = additions made after the original plan was written (session 2026-07-02 onward),
> including the full Phase 70–73 backend-perfection wave below.
> **WebGPU (Phases 56–69) lives in a separate file, [`plan_webgpu.md`](plan_webgpu.md)**,
> numbered `WEBGPU-1`–`WEBGPU-123` — moved out of this file entirely (2026-07-07) to keep the
> parked WebGPU work fully separate from this active backlog; WebGPU is deliberately
> deprioritized regardless — see "Execution order" below.
>
> **Completed tasks 1–500 live in a separate archive file,
> [`plan_graphics_20260708.md`](plan_graphics_20260708.md)**, moved out of this file (2026-07-08)
> to keep the active backlog manageable — no task content changed, only the file location. Every
> phase whose 1–500-numbered tasks are all done has its table replaced here with a one-line
> pointer to the archive; phases with any remaining work (whether numbered 1–500 or higher) keep
> their still-open rows in place.
>
> **A second archive, [`plan_graphics_20260709.md`](plan_graphics_20260709.md), holds phases
> whose remaining work (regardless of task-number range) was completed between 2026-07-08 and
> 2026-07-09** (Phase 30, Phase 47's own higher-numbered 664–665, Phase 48, Phase 50, Phase 52,
> and Phase 70), moved out the same way — same rule: a phase's table is replaced with a one-line
> pointer only once every row it currently shows here is ✅; a phase with any open row keeps its
> full table in place regardless of how many of its other rows are already done.

> ⛔ **WEBGPU IS FORBIDDEN FOR NOW.** Do not work on anything in `plan_webgpu.md` — do not
> start, stub, scaffold, or plan implementation — until the project owner explicitly lifts this
> restriction. This is a hard prohibition, not just a priority/ordering note; see `CLAUDE.md`
> ("WebGPU Is Forbidden For Now"), which is authoritative. When working through this file
> autonomously, `plan_webgpu.md` is entirely out of scope.

## Execution order (priority, not document order)

1. **Phases 1–55** (core, Tasks 1–663) — in progress, ~44% done as of 2026-07-02.
2. **Phase 70 — SDL_Renderer** (Tasks 666–731) — the 2D-only backend must reach verified
   perfection first: it's the smallest, simplest surface, and every other backend's SpriteBatch/
   Texture2D work builds on the same XNA semantics SDL_Renderer has to get exactly right.
3. **Phase 71 — EasyGL final gap closure** (Tasks 732–739) — EasyGL is already the de facto
   target of most unlabeled tasks in Phases 34–55, so this phase is deliberately small: it only
   covers what genuinely isn't tracked elsewhere yet (real `SurfaceFormat` GPU forwarding,
   `FillMode::WireFrame` emulation, mip-chain generation for `Texture3D`/`TextureCube`).
4. **Phase 72 — Bgfx parity** (Tasks 740–824) — full pixel-verified breadth, replicated from
   Phases 35–50's EasyGL-default testing matrix, starting from wiring up the pixel-readback path
   (`GetBackBufferData` exists per Task 117 but no current Bgfx test calls it).
5. **Phase 73 — Vulkan parity** (Tasks 664–665, 825–861) — Vulkan already has more infrastructure
   done than Bgfx (per-slot samplers, instancing, wireframe, MSAA, RenderTargetCube, stock-effect
   SPIR-V shaders), so this phase is gap-closure sized, not a full replication — plus the two
   SpriteBatch bugs found 2026-07-02 (multi-batch, dropped `SamplerState`).
6. **WebGPU** (`plan_webgpu.md`, Phases 56–69, `WEBGPU-1`+) — parked. Revisit only after 1–5
   above are done.

> Phases 70–73 physically appear after Phase 55 (Task 663) in this document (append-only) but
> run **before** the now-relocated WebGPU work — see the priority order above, not the phase
> numbers, for actual execution sequence.

## Phase 9 — Effect system generalization (critical gap)

> **Root cause:** `GraphicsDevice::currentEffect_` is typed `BasicEffect*`.
> `BuildGpuDrawParams()` takes `const BasicEffect*`.
> `AlphaTestEffect`, `DualTextureEffect`, `EnvironmentMapEffect`, and `SkinnedEffect`
> all inherit from `Effect`, not from `BasicEffect` — they cannot be passed to `DrawPrimitivesEx`.
> Their `OnApply()` fully updates `EffectParameter` objects, but GPU output is zero.

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|

> **All tasks in this phase are complete — moved to `plan_graphics_20260708.md`.**

## Phase 33 — Texture3D and TextureCube completeness

| #   | Task                                                                     | Status | Notes                                  |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------ | -------------------------------------- |
| 663 | Implement `TextureCube::DDSFromStreamEXT` for real (Task 272 finding)    | ✅      | **Real implementation, built and pixel/data-verified against a hand-built DDS fixture (no real .dds asset available in this environment, per this row's own "build a real DDS cube-map test fixture first" guidance).** Parses the DDS header byte-for-byte mirroring FNA's `Texture.ParseDDS` (magic/header-size/flags/height/width/mipMapCount/pixelformat/caps/caps2), throwing `System::FormatException("This file does not contain cube data!")` when `caps2`'s `DDSCAPS2_CUBEMAP` bit is absent (matches FNA's exact exception type/message) and `System::NotSupportedException` for malformed/unsupported input (bad magic, wrong header size, non-DXT1/3/5 fourCC — matching FNA's own `NotSupportedException` usage for equivalent cases). Decodes each of the 6 faces' `levels` DXT1/3/5-compressed mip levels to RGBA8 via the existing `DxtUtil::DecompressDxt{1,3,5}` (already used by `Texture2D::FromStream`), then uploads via `TextureCube::SetData`. **Deliberate CNA deviation from FNA, matching an already-established precedent for this exact class of problem**: FNA uploads the compressed DXT bytes directly to a real compressed-format GPU texture; CNA fully decompresses to RGBA8 on the CPU and always constructs the result as `SurfaceFormat::Color` instead, mirroring `Texture2D::FromStream`'s own identical, already-accepted choice (CNA doesn't implement compressed GPU texture formats end-to-end on any backend — see NEXT.md's documented "SurfaceFormat support is Color-only for real GPU formats" limitation). Only DXT1/3/5 are supported (no DX10 extended header / uncompressed / HDR variants) — same deliberate scope as `Texture2D::FromStream`'s own DDS support. **Found and fixed a real, separate infrastructure gap while writing this**: `TextureCube` had a user-declared destructor but no explicit move constructor/assignment, and its `unique_ptr` backend member made copy implicitly deleted — meaning `return result;` from a factory-style `static TextureCube XxxFromStreamEXT(...)` function couldn't compile at all (NRVO isn't guaranteed, and no move path existed as a fallback). Added explicit `TextureCube(TextureCube&&) noexcept = default`/`operator=(TextureCube&&) noexcept = default` plus explicit `= delete` copy ctor/assignment (documenting the class's actual by-design non-copyable, movable-only semantics). **Test fixture**: `BuildSolidColorCubeDds()` (in `TextureCubeTests.cpp`) hand-constructs a minimal, valid, 4×4, single-mip-level DXT1 cube-map DDS entirely in memory — one of 6 maximally-distinct, exactly-RGB565-representable solid colours per face (each channel already 0 or 255, so DXT1 round-trips it exactly with zero tolerance needed), each face encoded as one solid 8-byte DXT1 block (`color0==color1==target`, all 2-bit indices `0` — confirmed via reading `DxtUtil::DecompressDxt1Block` that index 0 always selects `color0` regardless of DXT1's internal `c0>c1` comparison branch), wrapped in a `System::IO::MemoryStream`. 6 new gtest cases in `TextureCubeTests.cpp`: empty-stream/bad-magic/non-cube/unsupported-fourCC all throw the correct exception type; size/format/levelCount decode correctly; **all 6 faces round-trip their exact distinct colour** — the last check is EasyGL-only for the strict per-channel assertion (`#ifdef CNA_BACKEND_EASYGL`), since `Texture3D`/`TextureCube::GetData` is a known, already-tracked no-op on Vulkan/Bgfx (Task 865, confirmed by this exact test failing there with all-zero readback before the guard was added — unrelated to DDS loading, not fixed here); Vulkan/Bgfx still exercise the full decode+upload path and confirm no crash. **A second, separate, real gap found and NOT fixed here** (out of scope, flagged for the backlog): `TextureCube::SetData`/`GetData`'s 6-arg overload never validates that the caller's `elementCount` actually matches the `w*h` region being read/written — confirmed via a genuine heap corruption (`free(): invalid pointer`) when an early draft of the new test passed `elementCount=1` for a 4×4=16-texel face read. Split to new **Task 913**. **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted `TextureCube.cpp` back to the old stub (with the header/test changes kept, exercising the exact same 6 new tests) — all 6 failed exactly as predicted (5 exception-type mismatches + the face-colour mismatch); restored and reconfirmed 6/6 PASS on all 3 backends. Full regression: `CnaTests` all 3 backends exact baseline match +6 (EasyGL/Vulkan 4270/4270 passed 2 skipped; Bgfx 4274/4274 passed 2 skipped), 0 failed. See `AUDIT.md` "TextureCube detailed audit" finding #6 (updated). |
| 913 | Fix `TextureCube::SetData`/`GetData`'s 6-arg overload: `elementCount` is never validated against the actual `w*h` region size, causing heap corruption on mismatch (found while building Task 663's DDS test fixture) | ✅ | **CLOSED — real memory-safety bug fixed exactly per this row's own predicted shape, and expanded to fix the identical gap in `Texture3D` too (same root cause, same one-line fix, found while reviewing this row's own sibling classes).** Confirmed FNA's real validation contract by reading `Texture2D.SetData<T>`/`GetData<T>` in the FNA reference source: `if (requiredBytes > availableBytes) throw ArgumentOutOfRangeException` — i.e. `elementCount` must be `>=` the region's texel count (extra data is allowed and ignored), not `==`. CNA's own `Texture2D.cpp` already implements this exact check (`if (elementCount < w * h) throw std::out_of_range(...)`) for both `SetData`/`GetData` — `TextureCube` and `Texture3D` were the only 2 sibling classes missing it. Added the identical `elementCount < w*h` (TextureCube) / `elementCount < (right-left)*(bottom-top)*(back-front)` (Texture3D) check to all 4 sites (`TextureCube::SetData`/`GetData`, `Texture3D::SetData`/`GetData`), matching `Texture2D.cpp`'s exact message wording style. 4 new gtest cases (`TextureCubeTests.cpp`/`Texture3DTests.cpp`): a rect/box requesting more texels/voxels than the supplied `elementCount` now throws `std::out_of_range` cleanly on all 3 backends. **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted both production files, reran the exact 4 new tests — all 4 failed (no exception thrown, silently proceeding into the same underage-buffer condition; did not reliably reproduce the original crash at this smaller 4-vs-16-byte delta, consistent with heap corruption being undefined behavior rather than a guaranteed crash — the "silently proceeds when it should throw" failure mode is itself the correct, confirmed regression signal); restored and reconfirmed 4/4 PASS on all 3 backends. Full regression: `CnaTests` all 3 backends exact baseline match +4 new tests, 0 failed. |
| 862 | Fix `Texture3D` mip levels >0: `EasyGLTexture3DBackend` constructor only allocates level 0 (Task 276 finding) | ✅ | **Confirmed and fixed — reproduced first, per this row's own "not yet verified with a reproducing test" note.** New backend-agnostic `examples/easygl_texture3d_mip_test.cpp` (4x4x4 `Texture3D`, `mipMap=true` → 3 levels: 4x4x4/2x2x2/1x1x1, mirrors Task 276's `TextureCube` mip test shape) initially FAILED exactly as predicted: mip levels 1/2 read back `(0,0,0)` instead of their written colour. Root cause identical to Task 276: `EasyGLTexture3DBackend`'s constructor called `set_image_3d` once for level 0 only, no level loop; `SetData` writes via `set_sub_image_3d` (`glTexSubImage3D`), which requires the target level to already have a defined image. Fixed by pre-allocating every mip level in the constructor (new `CalculateTexture3DMipLevels(w,h)` + a per-level `set_image_3d(nullptr)` loop, each level also halving `depth` — the depth dimension doesn't participate in the level *count* per FNA's real `Texture3D` constructor, but each level's own GPU storage still halves in all 3 dimensions, standard volume-mip behavior). All 73 checks (64 mip0 + 8 mip1 + 1 mip2) now pass. Registered for EasyGL as `EasyGL_Texture3D_Mip_RoundTrip` and reused unmodified for Vulkan (Task 864) as `Vulkan_Texture3D_Mip_RoundTrip`, since the test file has zero backend-specific code. **Discriminating power independently verified**: ran the identical compiled test binary against the pre-fix backend build first (all 64 mip0 checks passed, all 9 mip1/mip2 checks failed exactly as predicted above), then rebuilt with the fix applied and reconfirmed 73/73 PASS. Full regression: `CnaTests` (EasyGL) 4272/4274 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match, new test is ctest-only); `ctest -R "Texture3D\|TextureCube"` (EasyGL) 88/88. |
| 863 | Wire `Texture3D` sampling into shaders (stock/custom effects) — currently structurally impossible (Task 277 finding) | ⬜ | **NEEDS_HUMAN (2026-07-09 backlog-hygiene pass) — this row's own text already identifies the blocker as "a real architecture decision" between 2 named options, neither picked; `NEXT.md` §9 independently flags this as "No architecture change... without a deliberate, scoped design pass — it touches EffectParameter, TextureCollection, and every backend's texture-bind code. Not a small patch." Confirmed still accurate — not attempted, needs the project owner to pick option (a) or (b) below before any code changes.** FNA's `Texture3D : Texture` lets any texture (2D/3D/Cube) go into `GraphicsDevice.Textures[slot]` and be sampled by a shader; CNA's `Texture3D : GraphicsResource` (not `Texture`) cannot be assigned into `TextureCollection` at all, and `ShaderEffect`/`IEffectBackend` have no texture-binding API whatsoever (only scalar/vector/matrix uniforms) for any texture type, so there is no custom-effect workaround either. `EffectParameter::SetValue(Texture3D*)`/`GetValueTexture3D()` exist but have zero consumers in any backend — a write-only dead end. Fix requires a real architecture decision: (a) make `Texture3D` (and `TextureCube`, same issue — see Task 278) inherit `Texture` to match FNA and unify `TextureCollection` handling, likely a non-trivial refactor touching `EffectParameter`, `TextureCollection`, and every backend's texture-bind code; or (b) add a parallel, `Texture3D`-specific GPU-binding path outside `TextureCollection`. Out of scope for the Task 277/278 verify-only audits. See `AUDIT.md`. |
| 864 | Fix Vulkan and Bgfx mip-level allocation for `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` — `mipLevels`/`hasMips` hardcoded to 1/false regardless of `mipMap` (Task 280 finding) | ✅ | **Vulkan half fixed and pixel-verified; Bgfx half split to new Task 914 (genuinely blocked, not attempted — see below).** Confirmed exactly as predicted: `VulkanGraphicsBackend::CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` took `bool /*mipMap*/` and dropped it entirely; `VulkanTexture3DBackend`/`VulkanTextureCubeBackend`'s `VkImageCreateInfo::mipLevels` was hardcoded to `1`. Fixed by threading `mipMap` all the way through both constructors, computing the real level count (`CalculateVulkanTexture3DMipLevels(w,h)`/`CalculateVulkanTextureCubeMipLevels(size)`, mirroring `Texture3D.cpp`/`TextureCube.cpp`'s own formulas exactly) and using it for `VkImageCreateInfo::mipLevels`, the sampling `VkImageView`'s `subresourceRange.levelCount`, and a new construction-time full-mip-range initial layout transition (a manual multi-level `VkImageMemoryBarrier`, since the existing shared `TransitionImageLayout` helper hardcodes a single-level range — mirrors `VulkanRenderTargetBackend`/`VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend`'s identical Task 878/907 fix shape exactly). **Found a striking, more severe symptom than a simple no-op**: reverting the fix and calling `SetData(level=1,...)`/`SetData(level=2,...)` against an image with only 1 mip level actually allocated didn't just silently fail — it **corrupted mip level 0's real GPU memory** (confirmed via the discriminating-power check below: post-revert, `mip0 texel 0/1` read back mip2's orange colour instead of red, and `mip1` reads returned mip2's orange too), consistent with the out-of-range mip-level copy region landing back inside the image's only real allocated memory range on this driver. Verified via 2 tests: the existing, unmodified `easygl_texturecube_mip_test.cpp` (Task 276) registered for Vulkan for the first time as `Vulkan_TextureCube_Mip_RoundTrip` (126/126 pass — only possible now because Task 865 made Vulkan's `GetData` real), and a **new** backend-agnostic `examples/easygl_texture3d_mip_test.cpp` (Task 862/864, 4x4x4 `Texture3D`, 3 levels) registered for both EasyGL (closing Task 862, see above) and Vulkan (73/73 pass on both). **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted only the 2 Vulkan backend files (test/CMake registrations kept) — reran both tests, confirmed genuine failures (`Texture3D` 67/73 pass with 6 corrupted, `TextureCube` 112/126 pass with 14 corrupted, matching the memory-corruption symptom above, not a clean "levels 1+ untouched" pattern); restored and reconfirmed 73/73 + 126/126 PASS. Full regression: `CnaTests` (Vulkan) 4272/4274 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); `ctest -R "Texture3D\|TextureCube"` (Vulkan) 85/85. **Bgfx split to Task 914**: applying the identical mechanical fix (`hasMips` threaded through `bgfx::createTexture3D`/`createTextureCube`) is straightforward and low-risk by inspection, but this project's own discriminating-power-verification rule can't currently be satisfied on Bgfx — confirmed empirically that Bgfx's `Texture3D`/`TextureCube::GetData` is a total no-op (no override exists at all, unlike Vulkan's real Task 865 implementation), so the existing mip test just reads back untouched grey for every check regardless of whether the fix is applied (0 crash, 100% FAIL either way — not discriminating). The only other verification path, `EnvironmentMapEffect`+forced-minification sampling, was already independently found non-discriminating for cube-map mip *content* specifically by Task 907's own `bgfx_rendertargetcube_mip_test.cpp` (same object type, same sampling path) — and `Texture3D` can't be sampled in any shader at all (Task 863, a separate already-tracked architectural gap), so there is currently no way to verify the `Texture3D` half on Bgfx by any means. Not fixed here to avoid landing an unverified change; see Task 914 for the fix shape and a real path forward (bgfx does have a real `bgfx::readTexture()` readback API, gated behind `BGFX_TEXTURE_READ_BACK` and async multi-frame turnaround — implementing it is a genuinely separate, nontrivial architecture change, not a 1-line mirror of this fix). |
| 914 | Bgfx: implement a real GPU readback path for `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` (via `bgfx::readTexture()`), then apply Task 864's mip-allocation fix to Bgfx | ✅ | **CLOSED — `Texture3D`/`TextureCube::GetData` is now real on Bgfx (previously a total no-op, the shared `ITextureCubeBackend`/`ITexture3DBackend` default silently left the caller's buffer untouched), and Task 864's mip-allocation fix (`mipMap` genuinely threaded through `_hasMips`, previously hardcoded `false`) now applies to Bgfx too, verifiable for the first time.** `bgfx::readTexture()` requires its SOURCE texture to be created with `BGFX_TEXTURE_READ_BACK`, which is incompatible with a normally shader-sampled texture ("can't be a GPU resource at the same time" per bgfx's own doc comment) — so `GetData()` instead creates a small temporary texture sized exactly to the requested region, flagged `BGFX_TEXTURE_BLIT_DST | BGFX_TEXTURE_READ_BACK` (a plain 2D texture for `TextureCube` — cube faces address via `bgfx::blit()`'s own `_srcZ` face-index parameter into a 2D destination per its doc comment; a 3D volume texture for `Texture3D`, keeping both sides symmetric), blits the requested face/mip/region into it via `bgfx::blit()`, then reads that temporary texture directly into the caller's buffer via `bgfx::readTexture()` — created and destroyed per call (`GetData` is not a hot path). `bgfx::readTexture()` is asynchronous (returns a future frame number, not an immediate result); a new shared `AdvanceFramesUntil()` helper loops `bgfx::frame()` up to 4 times until that frame number is reached, mirroring `ReadBackbuffer`'s own already-established retry-until-ready convention for bgfx's inherently deferred completion model exactly (same up-to-N-attempts shape, same accepted trade-off of forcing extra frame boundaries mid-call). **Confirmed both `BGFX_CAPS_TEXTURE_BLIT` and `BGFX_CAPS_TEXTURE_READ_BACK` are actually supported in this project's Xvfb/llvmpipe/OpenGL 2.1 sandbox** via a throwaway diagnostic caps-log check before implementing anything (reverted before committing) — this was the real open question the original Task 864 scoping note flagged, and it resolved in favor of full implementability here. Registered the same 4 backend-agnostic example files already shared by EasyGL/Vulkan for Bgfx too (Tasks 173/275 `GetData` round-trips, 862/864 mip-allocation tests) rather than writing new ones — all 4 pass on the first run. **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash`**: reverting both backend files reproduced the exact predicted failures on all 4 new tests (0/16, 2/116, 0/73, 0/126 — the 2 stray `Texture3D_Slices`/`TextureCube_PartialRect` passes are the tests' own baseline-state assertions made before any `GetData` call, not false negatives); restored and reconfirmed all 4 pass 100%. Full Bgfx regression: `ctest` 4342/4344 passed (2 pre-existing: `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/`MipChain`, the same already-documented Xvfb/environment limitations this project has carried since Task 750/877 — zero new failures). |
| 915 | Implement `SdlGraphicsBackend::ReadBackbuffer` (`GetBackBufferData`) — foundational prerequisite for the SDL_Renderer pixel-test audit phase (Task 666) | ✅ | **Real, severe, previously-undiscovered gap found and fixed while starting Task 666's audit phase.** `SdlGraphicsBackend` never overrode `IGraphicsBackend::ReadBackbuffer` at all — the shared default throws `std::runtime_error("ReadBackbuffer: not implemented in this backend")`, meaning `GraphicsDevice::GetBackBufferData` (the pixel-verification mechanism every single EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx test in this project relies on) was structurally impossible on SDL_Renderer, blocking the entire Tasks 666–861 audit phase before any of it could start. Fixed via `SDL_RenderReadPixels()` + `SDL_ConvertSurface(..., SDL_PIXELFORMAT_RGBA32)` for a consistent byte layout, copying row-by-row (respecting `pitch`) into the caller's tightly-packed buffer. **Found a real, non-obvious subtlety while implementing it**: `SDL_RenderReadPixels` operates in *physical* output coordinates, but every other backend (and `GetViewportSize()`'s own contract) works in *logical* (virtual-resolution) coordinates — and this project's default presentation mode, `CnaPresentationMode::FixedHeightDynamicWidth`, deliberately does NOT map logical pixels 1:1 to physical pixels (it derives a dynamic logical width from the window's real aspect ratio at a fixed logical height, confirmed empirically: a requested 64×64 canvas became a real `107×64` logical size against an 800×480 physical window). `SdlGraphicsBackend::ReadBackbuffer` detects this via `SDL_GetRenderLogicalPresentationRect()` and throws a clear error if physical/logical sizes don't match, rather than silently returning wrong/aliased data; when a custom render target is bound (`SDL_GetRenderTarget(renderer) != nullptr`), logical presentation doesn't apply at all, so no mapping is needed. Test-side, this means exact-pixel tests must explicitly request `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` (maps to `SDL_LOGICAL_PRESENTATION_DISABLED`) to get true 1:1 correspondence — confirmed working via direct experimentation before landing the final test. New `examples/sdlrenderer_readback_test.cpp` (SpriteBatch-drawn red square over a green background, reads one pixel inside and one outside) plus a brand-new `cna_sdl_test` CMake macro/test-registration block (backend never had one before). **Found and fixed a second, unrelated, real CMakeLists.txt bug while wiring this up**: the new test block was initially inserted inside `if(CNA_BUILD_EXAMPLES AND (CNA_GRAPHICS_BACKEND STREQUAL "EASYGL" OR CNA_GRAPHICS_BACKEND STREQUAL "VULKAN"))` (a ~2850-line block spanning lines 579–3436) without realizing the whole span was gated to those 2 backends only — confirmed via bisection with temporary `message()`/`FATAL_ERROR` markers that the block was silently skipped for SDL_Renderer (and would be for Bgfx too) at configure time, with `cmake -S . -B` still reporting "Configuring done" successfully despite an unconditional `FATAL_ERROR` inside never firing. Not a pre-existing bug — the block's own scoping is intentional (EasyGL/Vulkan-only 3D-demo/test registrations) — just a wrong insertion point on my part; moved to right after the mega-block's true close (line 3436), alongside the existing, correctly-scoped `BGFX`-only test block. **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted both backend files — the pre-fix binary aborted with the exact predicted `std::runtime_error("ReadBackbuffer: not implemented in this backend")`; restored and reconfirmed both checks pass. Full regression: `CnaTests` (SDL_Renderer, filtered) 4262/4274 passed, 2 skipped, 10 failed (all pre-existing/expected — `EffectApplyTest`/`GraphicsDeviceValidationTest`/`SkinnedModelEXTPartTest`, all throwing `"SDL_Renderer does not support 3D"`, matching this backend's already-documented, accepted 2D-only architectural scope); the full unfiltered `ctest` run (4339 tests, including `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest.*`, first time this backend's complete suite was run this systematically) found 3 more failures in that same excluded suite, confirmed via direct rerun to throw the identical `"SDL_Renderer does not support 3D"` message — same root cause, not a new regression, bringing the real established SDL_Renderer baseline to 13 known/expected 3D-related failures out of 4339, 0 unexplained; `SDL_Renderer_Readback` itself passes cleanly. EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx builds spot-checked clean (configure + target-count sanity check) after the CMakeLists.txt restructuring. |
| 865 | Implement real GPU readback for `Texture3D`/`TextureCube::GetData` on Vulkan (Task 280 finding) | ✅ | **Fixed — real `vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer` + host-visible staging-buffer readback for both `VulkanTexture3DBackend::GetData` and `VulkanTextureCubeBackend::GetData`, mirroring `SetData`'s upload path in reverse.** `Texture3D::GetData` allocates one staging buffer (`VK_BUFFER_USAGE_TRANSFER_DST_BIT`, `HOST_VISIBLE\|HOST_COHERENT`), transitions the source image `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL -> TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL` via the shared `TransitionImageLayout` helper, issues `vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer` for the requested `level`/`x,y,z`/`w,h,depth` region, transitions back to `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL`, then `memcpy`s from the mapped staging buffer into the caller's output. `TextureCube::GetData` follows the identical shape but can't use the shared `TransitionImageLayout` helper (it only targets array layer 0), so it builds its own inline `VkImageMemoryBarrier` pair scoped to `subresourceRange = {level, 1, face, 1}` before/after the copy, addressing the requested `face` directly. **Two prerequisite fixes required for this to work at all**: (1) `TransitionImageLayout` had no case for `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL <-> TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL` (only `UNDEFINED/TRANSFER_DST_OPTIMAL/SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` transitions existed, since nothing previously read a texture back after upload) — added both directions with the correct `VK_ACCESS_SHADER_READ_BIT`/`VK_ACCESS_TRANSFER_READ_BIT` access masks and `FRAGMENT_SHADER`/`TRANSFER` pipeline stages. (2) Both `VulkanTexture3DBackend` and `VulkanTextureCubeBackend`'s `VkImageCreateInfo::usage` only had `TRANSFER_DST_BIT \| SAMPLED_BIT` (upload-and-sample only, no readback path); added `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_TRANSFER_SRC_BIT` to both. **Test approach**: rather than writing new test files, registered two existing backend-agnostic example tests (previously EasyGL-only) for Vulkan in `CMakeLists.txt` — `examples/easygl_texture3d_slices_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip` (4 z-slices × 4 pixels, per-voxel colour verified) and `examples/easygl_texturecube_partial_rect_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_TextureCube_PartialRect_RoundTrip` (partial-rect sub-tests A/B/C across all 6 faces) — both pass 100% real per-pixel/per-voxel verification, no argument-guard-only coverage. Also updated `TextureCubeTests.cpp`'s `DDSFromStreamEXTDecodesAllSixFacesWithDistinctColours` guard from `#ifdef CNA_BACKEND_EASYGL` to `#if defined(CNA_BACKEND_EASYGL) \|\| defined(CNA_BACKEND_VULKAN)`, since Vulkan's `GetData` is now real too (Bgfx remains excluded — no GPU readback API at all, accepted project-wide limitation). **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted only the two Vulkan backend files (leaving the test-file guard change and CMakeLists.txt registrations in place) — confirmed genuine failures: 16/16 FAIL in `Vulkan_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip`, 114/114 FAIL in `Vulkan_TextureCube_PartialRect_RoundTrip`, and the DDS 6-face test failed on `face 5`'s colour mismatch — exactly the buffer-untouched symptom this task describes. Restored via `git stash pop`, reconfirmed all 3 pass. Full regression: `CnaTests` all 3 backends — Vulkan 4272/4274 passed (2 skipped, pre-existing/unrelated), EasyGL 4272/4274 passed (2 skipped), Bgfx 4276/4278 passed (2 skipped) — 0 failures on any backend; `ctest -R "Texture3D\|TextureCube"` on Vulkan: 83/83 passed. |
| 866 | Set `Name` on `BlendState`/`DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState` static presets, matching FNA (Task 291 finding) | ✅ | Task 291's `SamplerState` audit found FNA's private preset constructor sets `Name` (e.g. `"SamplerState.PointClamp"`) on every preset; CNA's `SamplerState` didn't, and was fixed under Task 291. Checking sibling state classes during that audit found the identical gap in all 3 of `BlendState`/`DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState`. **`BlendState`'s portion fixed under Task 301**, **`DepthStencilState`'s portion fixed under Task 311**, **`RasterizerState`'s portion fixed under Task 321** (all their own natural audit tasks), matching Task 291's fix shape exactly. **Fully closed** — all 4 state classes (`SamplerState`/`BlendState`/`DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState`) now correctly set `Name` on every preset. |
| 867 | Implement real GPU upload for `Texture2D::SetData(level>0,...)` on Vulkan and Bgfx, and fix EasyGL's mip-incomplete-texture black-screen bug (Task 298/299 findings) | ✅ | **Split (2026-07-09) into 3 single-backend sub-tasks, mirroring this project's established precedent for large multi-backend fixes (Task 868→923, Task 878→899) — this row itself is now just a tracking pointer, not attempted as one unit.** Task 924 (EasyGL `GL_TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL` fix), Task 925 (Vulkan real mip upload + allocation fix), and Task 926 (Bgfx real mip upload, including a mutable-texture restructuring) are all done — the full 3-way split is closed. See their own rows below. **Severe, silent-failure bug, same class as Task 865.** `ITextureBackend::UpdatePixelsLevel(level, rgba, w, h)` (the virtual hook `Texture2D::SetData(level, rect, data, ...)` calls for `level>0`) has an empty no-op default body in `include/CNA/Internal/Backends/Common/IGraphicsBackend.hpp`, and **neither `VulkanTextureBackend` nor the Bgfx texture backend overrides it** — confirmed by grep, zero matches in either backend's `.cpp`. Calling `Texture2D::SetData(level>0, ...)` on a mipmapped `Texture2D` on Vulkan or Bgfx silently does nothing; the mip level's GPU content is whatever it was at texture creation (uninitialized/zero), with no error of any kind. Only EasyGL implements this correctly (confirmed working via Task 171's existing `easygl_texture2d_mip_test.cpp` and this session's new Task 298 test). Found while building Task 298's mipmap-filter pixel test: the Vulkan run showed the expected-high-mip-level colour never appearing, which traced back to this — not a filter/sampler issue at all, a data-upload issue. **Additional, related findings needed for a complete fix on Vulkan specifically** (found while tracing this): `VulkanTextureBackend`'s `VkImageCreateInfo::mipLevels` is hardcoded to `1` (same bug shape as Task 864's `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` finding, but for `Texture2D` — previously undocumented), its `VkImageViewCreateInfo::subresourceRange.levelCount` is also hardcoded to `1` (so even a real multi-level image wouldn't be visible past level 0 through this view), and `VulkanGraphicsBackend::ApplySamplerState`'s `VkSamplerCreateInfo` never sets `minLod`/`maxLod` (both default to `0.0`, clamping the shader's automatic LOD selection to level 0 regardless of `mipmapMode`) — all three must be fixed together, not just `UpdatePixelsLevel`, for Vulkan `Texture2D` mips to work end-to-end. Bgfx's fix shape is unconfirmed beyond the missing `UpdatePixelsLevel` override — not investigated further this session. **Task 299 found a related, EasyGL-specific finding from the same root cause** (Texture2D mip-level metadata never threaded into backend resource creation, manifesting differently per backend): `TextureFilter::Anisotropic` (and every other `*Mip*`-suffixed `TextureFilter` value — `LinearMipPoint`, `PointMipLinear`, all four `Min*Mag*Mip*` combinations) maps to a GL minFilter with a `_MIPMAP_` suffix (e.g. `LinearMipmapLinear`), which OpenGL requires a *complete* mipmap chain for; since EasyGL never sets `GL_TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL` to match each texture's real level count (confirmed via `easygl_texture_anisotropic_effect_test.cpp`: assigning `TextureFilter::Anisotropic` to an ordinary single-level `Texture2D::CreateFromPixels` texture — the overwhelmingly common case for real game textures — renders **solid black**, a classic GL mipmap-incompleteness symptom). Vulkan does not share this specific symptom (confirmed via the same test: renders correctly even on a single-level texture) — its issue is purely the missing `maxLod`/`mipLevels`/`levelCount` plumbing above, not incompleteness. Fix shape: for Vulkan/Bgfx, mirror the existing level-0 upload path (staging buffer for Vulkan, `bgfx::updateTexture2D` for Bgfx) generalized to arbitrary `level`, plus the three Vulkan-side allocation/view/sampler fixes above; for EasyGL, set `GL_TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL` on every `Texture2D` at creation time to match its real level count (1 for non-mipmapped, `CalculateMipLevels(w,h)-1` for mipmapped), which requires threading `mipMap`/level-count into `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::CreateTexture` (currently takes no such parameter, unlike `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube`). Not fixed here — this is a multi-part, three-backend-touching fix needing its own dedicated task and full regression pass, not something to bundle into a pixel-test task. |
| 924 | Fix EasyGL's mip-incomplete-texture black-screen bug: set `GL_TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL` on every `Texture2D` to match its real level count (split from Task 867) | ✅ | **CLOSED — real bug fixed exactly per Task 867's own predicted fix shape.** Added `int mipLevels = 1;` to the shared `CNA::Internal::Graphics::ImageData` struct (a new default-valued field, not a signature change — every existing call site across all 4 backends' `CreateTexture(const ImageData&)` overrides stays source-compatible unchanged) and set it to the real `levelCount_` at the 3 `Texture2D.cpp` call sites where it can legitimately differ from the default 1: the `mipMap`-aware constructor (`Texture2D(device,w,h,mipMap,format)`), and the 2 `SetData` codepaths that fully recreate the backend (`SetData(Color*,int)`'s unconditional recreate, and `SetData(level,rect,data,...)`'s level-0 lazy-attach-when-`backend_==nullptr` path) — both needed it so an already-mipmapped texture doesn't silently get `GL_TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL` reset back to 0 the next time either path fires. `EasyGLTextureBackend` gained a new `mipLevels_` member (read at construction, reused by `recreate_gl_resource()` after a context-loss recovery) and calls the already-existing `texture.set_parameter(TextureTarget::Texture2D, TextureParameter::MaxLevel, mipLevels_ - 1)` (both `TextureParameter::MaxLevel`/`GL_TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL` and `easygl::Texture::set_parameter` already existed in the vendored `meta-gl`/`easy-gl` libraries — no cross-repo edit needed this time, unlike Task 918's `MaxAnisotropy` addition). New `EasyGL_Texture2D_AnisotropicSingleLevel` test reproduces this row's own exact repro (`Texture2D::CreateFromPixels`, `TextureFilter::Anisotropic`, no mip data ever uploaded) and asserts the sampled colour is NOT solid black. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting just the fix (keeping the new test) reproduced the exact predicted solid-black failure; restored and reconfirmed pass. Full EasyGL regression: `ctest` 4535/4539 passed (4 pre-existing: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`/Task 145, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`/Task 872, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`/sibling-repo, 1 unrelated `AudioCategoryTest` flake), zero new failures. Cross-backend compile check: Vulkan/Bgfx/SDL_Renderer all build clean (the new `ImageData::mipLevels` field is additive; only EasyGL's own `CreateTexture` implementation reads it). Vulkan (Task 925) and Bgfx (Task 926) real-mip-upload halves remain open. |
| 925 | Implement real GPU mip-level upload + allocation + sampler LOD fix for `Texture2D` on Vulkan (split from Task 867) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 3 of the original 4 sub-fixes were genuinely needed; the 4th (sampler `minLod`/`maxLod`) turned out to already be fixed, a stale finding in this row's own original diagnosis.** Confirmed `VulkanGraphicsBackend::ApplySamplerState`/`CreateSampler` both already set `ci.maxLod = VK_LOD_CLAMP_NONE` (with an existing comment crediting Task 878's own RT-mip prerequisite work) — Task 867's diagnosis predates that fix and was never re-checked; corrected here as a byproduct, not re-fixed. **Real fixes landed**: (1) `VulkanTextureBackend` gained a `levelCount_` member, threaded from the already-existing `ImageData::mipLevels` field (Task 924's own addition — no new signature change needed); (2) `VkImageCreateInfo::mipLevels`/`VkImageViewCreateInfo::subresourceRange.levelCount` now use `levelCount_` instead of hardcoded `1`; a new construction-time full-range barrier (mirroring `VulkanTexture3DBackend`'s Task 864 pattern) puts levels 1..N-1 into a defined layout before any real data reaches them; (3) `VulkanTextureBackend::UpdatePixelsLevel` implemented for real (staging buffer + `vkCmdCopyBufferToImage` targeting the caller's `level`, mirroring `VulkanTexture3DBackend::SetData`'s established shape) — previously the shared `IGraphicsBackend` no-op default, silently discarding the caller's mip data. **Found and fixed a genuine bug while verifying, not present in the original diagnosis**: naively reusing the shared single-level `TransitionImageLayout()` helper for `UpdatePixelsLevel(level>0)` (mirroring `VulkanTexture3DBackend::SetData`'s own existing, already-shipped Task 864 pattern) produces live Vulkan validation-layer errors — that helper always barriers level 0 regardless of which level is actually being copied, a genuine layout-tracking mismatch. Fixed with a new `VulkanTextureBackend::TransitionLevelLayout()` private method that barriers the real target level; confirmed the fix eliminates the validation errors entirely (0 remaining, previously 7 — one per level 1-7). **`VulkanTexture3DBackend`/`VulkanTextureCubeBackend`'s `SetData` likely have this identical latent imprecision** (not fixed here, out of this task's own `Texture2D`-only scope — worth a small dedicated follow-up if anyone hits it). Registered `Vulkan_TextureMipFilter_DualTextureEffect` (Task 298's own pixel test, previously explicitly NOT registered on Vulkan with a comment blaming this exact bug) — could not reuse the EasyGL source file verbatim: its own `TextureFilter::Point` check expects RED (Point never mip-selects), which is EasyGL-specific (`Point` maps to plain `GL_NEAREST`/`GL_NEAREST`, no `_MIPMAP_` suffix, a separate still-open limitation) — confirmed Vulkan's `ApplySamplerState` has ALWAYS mapped `Point` to `VK_SAMPLER_MIPMAP_MODE_NEAREST` (genuinely mip-aware, matching real XNA/D3D9 `TextureFilter::Point` semantics — point filtering on all 3 axes including mip selection, not "no mip selection"), so a Vulkan-specific test copy expects GREEN for both checks instead. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: both checks failed exactly as predicted (RED instead of GREEN) on the reverted code; restored and reconfirmed both pass, 0 validation errors. Full Vulkan regression: `ctest` 4444/4448 passed (4 pre-existing: 3 `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` segfaults + 1 `Vulkan_DepthBias` sub-case, matching the already-established baseline exactly), zero new failures. Cross-backend compile check: EasyGL/Bgfx/SDL_Renderer untouched (this fix touches only `VulkanGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp` and reuses the already-shared `ImageData::mipLevels`). Task 926 (Bgfx) remains open. |
| 926 | Implement real GPU mip-level upload for `Texture2D` on Bgfx (split from Task 867) | ✅ | **CLOSED — the last of the 3 backends split from Task 867; closes the split in full.** Found a deeper prerequisite gap than the row's own diagnosis anticipated: `BgfxTextureBackend` never overrode `UpdatePixels` (level 0) OR `UpdatePixelsLevel` (level>0) at all — confirmed via `bgfx::createTexture2D`'s own doc comment ("If `_mem` is non-NULL, created texture will be immutable"), the constructor's existing code passed real pixel data as `_mem` at creation time, making the texture immutable and `bgfx::updateTexture2D()` invalid on it per bgfx's own API contract, regardless of level. **Fix**: constructor now creates the texture WITHOUT initial `_mem` (mutable, mirroring `BgfxTextureCubeBackend`'s already-established pattern) with `hasMips = (data.mipLevels > 1)` genuinely threaded through (previously hardcoded `false`), then immediately calls `bgfx::updateTexture2D(handle, 0, 0, ...)` for the real initial level-0 upload. Added real `UpdatePixels`/`UpdatePixelsLevel` overrides, both via `bgfx::updateTexture2D`, mirroring `BgfxTextureCubeBackend::SetData`'s exact established shape — previously the shared `IGraphicsBackend` no-op defaults, silently discarding both the common in-place level-0 update path and any mip-level data. Registered `Bgfx_TextureMipFilter_DualTextureEffect` (Task 298's own pixel test) — a Bgfx-specific adaptation of the EasyGL source, same reasoning as Task 925's Vulkan copy: Bgfx's `TextureFilter::Point` has always mapped to `BGFX_SAMPLER_MIN_POINT|MAG_POINT|MIP_POINT` (genuinely mip-aware, matching real XNA/D3D9 semantics), unlike EasyGL's still-flat mapping, so both checks correctly expect GREEN. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: both checks failed as predicted on the reverted code (`(0,0,0)` black instead of GREEN); restored and reconfirmed both pass on the first attempt. Full Bgfx regression: `ctest` 4415/4416 passed (1 pre-existing: `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`, the already-documented Xvfb/DRI3 limitation), zero new failures. Cross-backend compile check: EasyGL/Vulkan/SDL_Renderer untouched. **This closes Task 867's entire 3-way split (924 EasyGL/925 Vulkan/926 Bgfx), all done.** |
| 868 | Implement real per-`Blend`/`BlendFunction` blend-state mapping on Vulkan; fix separate-alpha/blend-function gaps on Bgfx (Task 304 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED (Vulkan half only — Bgfx's own narrower gap split out to new Task 923, see below) — the last of Task 500's original 5 confirmed bugs (921/922/918/916 already closed this session).** Confirmed the row's own diagnosis directly: `ApplyBlendState` discarded all 6 real parameters except the enable/disable boolean, and 9 separate pipeline-creation functions (`GetOrCreatePipeline3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineAlphaTest3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineDualTex3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineEnvMap3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineLitTextured3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineSkinned3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D`) each hardcoded `BlendState.NonPremultiplied`'s own equation whenever blending was enabled at all. **Fix**: added `ToVkBlendFactor`/`ToVkBlendOp` mapping functions (mirroring EasyGL's already-correct `ToEasyGLBlendFactor`/`ToEasyGLBlendEquation` tables exactly, confirmed against the real XNA `Blend`/`BlendFunction` enum ordinals) and a shared `FillBlendAttachmentState()` helper (mirroring the existing `FillDepthStencilState()` pattern) used by all 9 functions. A new `BlendKeyParams` struct (mirroring `DepthStencilKeyParams`) stores the real 6 values, threaded through `ApplyBlendState` → a new `blendParams_` member → a new `Pending3DDraw::blendParams` field (mirroring the existing `dsParams` field, since Vulkan defers 3D draws and the deferred-record-time pipeline selection needs the value captured at enqueue time, not read live) → all 9 pipeline-creation function signatures (each gained one new `const BlendKeyParams& blendParams = {}` parameter). **Pipeline cache key width problem, and how it was solved**: the existing `uint64_t` key (`Make3DKey`/`MakeExt3DKey` + `FoldDepthFormatIntoKey`) already used up through ~bit 52 once a depth `VkFormat` is folded in, leaving too little free width for the full 22-bit blend descriptor (4 bits × 4 `Blend` fields + 3 bits × 2 `BlendFunction` fields) needed for correctness across the full enum range — rather than risk bit-overlap corruption by cramming it in, all 9 pipeline caches were changed from `std::unordered_map<uint64_t, VkPipeline>` to `std::unordered_map<PipelineKey, VkPipeline, PipelineKeyHash>` where `PipelineKey = std::pair<uint64_t, uint32_t>` (a new `PackBlendBits()` computes the second half, collapsing to a canonical `0` whenever blending is disabled to avoid needless pipeline duplication across differently-disabled `BlendState`s) — a mechanical, zero-risk-to-the-existing-bits change, since `std::pair`'s own default `operator==` already does correct member-wise comparison; only a hash functor was needed. **Verification**: `git stash`-reverted both changed files, rebuilt, reran the 7 `Vulkan_BlendState_*` tests — the exact same 5 (`AlphaBlend`/`Additive`/`SeparateFunctions`/`SeparateFactors`/`BlendFactor`) failed with the exact previously-documented wrong values; restored and reconfirmed all 7 pass. **Full Vulkan integration-test subset** (`ctest -R "^Vulkan_"`): 92/93 passed — every one of the 5 known `BlendState` failures now passes; only the pre-existing, unrelated, already-tracked `Vulkan_DepthBias` sub-case remains (untouched by this task, out of scope). **Full Vulkan suite** (unit+integration): see the commit's own regression note below for the exact numbers. **Cross-backend compile check**: EasyGL/Bgfx/SDL_Renderer all build `CNA` clean — this fix touches only `VulkanGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp`. **Bgfx's own narrower gap (from this same row's original diagnosis) was deliberately NOT attempted here** — after completing Vulkan's own substantially larger fix, tackling Bgfx too in the same task risked a rushed, less-verified result; split into new **Task 923** instead (see its own row below), matching this project's established precedent for splitting large multi-backend tasks (e.g. Task 888→899). This closes the LAST of Task 500's 5 original confirmed bugs on Vulkan specifically — a new milestone assessment (if any) is a separate decision, not made here. Original diagnosis preserved below for full context. |

**Original diagnosis (preserved for context; superseded by the fix above for Vulkan, Task 923 tracks the remaining Bgfx gap):** **Massive, severe, previously undiscovered bug — Vulkan's blend state support is almost entirely fake.** `VulkanGraphicsBackend::ApplyBlendState` ignores every one of its 6 parameters (`colorSrcBlend`/`alphaSrcBlend`/`colorDstBlend`/`alphaDstBlend`/`colorBlendFunc`/`alphaBlendFunc`) except to compute a single boolean: `blendEnabled_ = !(colorSrcBlend==0 && colorDstBlend==1 && alphaSrcBlend==0 && alphaDstBlend==1)` (i.e. "is this exactly `BlendState.Opaque`'s values?"). Whenever that's false — `BlendState.Additive`, `AlphaBlend`, any custom `BlendState`, literally anything except `Opaque` — every pipeline hardcodes `srcColorBlendFactor=VK_BLEND_FACTOR_SRC_ALPHA`, `dstColorBlendFactor=VK_BLEND_FACTOR_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA`, `srcAlphaBlendFactor=VK_BLEND_FACTOR_ONE`, `dstAlphaBlendFactor=VK_BLEND_FACTOR_ZERO`, `colorBlendOp=alphaBlendOp=VK_BLEND_OP_ADD` (confirmed at 9 separate `cbs{}` pipeline-creation call sites in `VulkanGraphicsBackend.cpp` — this is baked into every pipeline variant, not one central place). That hardcoded equation happens to be exactly `BlendState.NonPremultiplied`'s — meaning `BlendState.AlphaBlend` (which requires `colorSrc=One`, premultiplied input) is silently rendered using `NonPremultiplied`'s equation instead, and `BlendState.Additive` (`colorSrc=SourceAlpha, colorDst=One`) is *also* silently rendered as `NonPremultiplied`. Found via Task 304's `AlphaBlend` pixel test: expected `R≈128` (correct premultiplied-input math), got `R=64` (exactly the double-alpha-multiplication signature of the wrong, hardcoded equation) — confirmed on real Vulkan hardware (AMD RADV), not a theoretical read of the code. **Bgfx has a narrower, more scoped gap** (found while cross-checking): `BgfxGraphicsBackend::ApplyBlendState`'s `XnaBlendToBgfxFactor` helper *does* correctly map all 12 `Blend` enum values individually, and color source/destination factors are applied correctly via `BGFX_STATE_BLEND_FUNC(src,dst)` — but `alphaSrcBlend`/`alphaDstBlend` are unused parameters (commented out in the signature), so the alpha channel always reuses the color channel's factors instead of its own, and `colorBlendFunc`/`alphaBlendFunc` (`BlendFunction`: `Add`/`Subtract`/`ReverseSubtract`/`Max`/`Min`) are entirely ignored — always implicitly `Add`. **EasyGL is fully, correctly implemented** for contrast/confirmation: `ApplyBlendState` uses `glBlendFuncSeparate` with a complete, correct per-value `Blend`→`GLenum` mapping for all 12 values, and `glBlendEquationSeparate` with a complete, correct `BlendFunction`→`GLenum` mapping for all 5 values, independently for color and alpha channels — this is why Task 304's EasyGL test passed exactly as predicted while Vulkan's failed. **This affects most/all of Phase 36's remaining Vulkan pixel tests** (306 `Additive`, 307 separate color/alpha functions, 308 separate blend factors, 309 `BlendFactor`/`MultiSampleMask`) — expect them to reveal the SAME root cause repeatedly on Vulkan until this is fixed; do not re-diagnose it as a new bug each time. **Update (Task 309):** confirmed a fifth time via `Vulkan_BlendState_BlendFactor` (`Blend::BlendFactor` never selects `VK_BLEND_FACTOR_CONSTANT_COLOR` in the hardcoded equation, so the correctly-set `vkCmdSetBlendConstants` value is moot on Vulkan) — no new information, same root cause. Task 309 also fixed the Bgfx-specific `BlendFactor`-propagation piece of Bgfx's narrower gap (`blendFactorPacked_` now passed to all 5 `bgfx::setState()` call sites); Bgfx's remaining gap — `alphaSrcBlend`/`alphaDstBlend` unused, `colorBlendFunc`/`alphaBlendFunc` always `Add` — is still open and unaffected by that fix. Fix shape for Vulkan: replace the single hardcoded `cba`/`cbs` blend-attachment setup (duplicated across ~9 pipeline-creation sites) with a real `Blend`→`VkBlendFactor` and `BlendFunction`→`VkBlendOp` mapping (mirroring EasyGL's/Bgfx's helper-function pattern), threading the actual `colorSrcBlend`/`alphaSrcBlend`/`colorDstBlend`/`alphaDstBlend`/`colorBlendFunc`/`alphaBlendFunc` values into every pipeline variant — likely needs the blend state baked into the existing per-pipeline cache key (mirroring how fill-mode/MSAA/depth already vary pipeline selection) since Vulkan pipelines fix blend state at creation time. Fix shape for Bgfx: thread `alphaSrcBlend`/`alphaDstBlend` through a `BGFX_STATE_BLEND_FUNC_SEPARATE`-style call, and map `colorBlendFunc`/`alphaBlendFunc` via `BGFX_STATE_BLEND_EQUATION_*`/`BGFX_STATE_BLEND_EQUATION_SEPARATE`. Not fixed here — large, correctness-critical, multi-pipeline-site change needing its own dedicated task and full regression pass on both backends. |
| 869 | `GraphicsDevice` state properties (`BlendState`/`DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState`) use value semantics instead of FNA's reference semantics (Task 310 finding) | ⬜ | **NEEDS_HUMAN (2026-07-09 backlog-hygiene pass) — this row's own analysis already recommends NOT pursuing this without a concrete game use case; flagging explicitly rather than silently leaving it open, since implementing it would be a real cross-cutting architecture change (`std::shared_ptr`-ifying 4 state classes project-wide) requiring the project owner's own authorization, not something to attempt opportunistically.** FNA's `GraphicsDevice.BlendState`/`DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState` setters store a **reference** to the assigned C# object (e.g. `nextBlend = value;`), since these are C# reference types — mutating that same object *after* assigning it to the device changes what's applied on the next `ApplyState()`/Draw, with no copy ever taken. CNA's `GraphicsDevice` stores all 3 (plus `SamplerStateCollection`'s per-slot `SamplerState`s) **by value** (`blendState_`/`depthStencilState_`/`rasterizerState_` are plain value members, confirmed via `GraphicsDevice.hpp`) — `setBlendStateProperty`/equivalent setters copy the struct rather than aliasing it, so mutating the original object post-assignment is silently a no-op on the device's already-applied copy, unlike real XNA/FNA. This is a deliberate, consistent, project-wide architectural pattern, not a `BlendState`-specific oversight — confirmed no game/example code in this repo relies on post-assignment mutation taking effect. New test `GraphicsDeviceDefaultStateTests.cpp::MutatingBlendStateAfterAssignmentDoesNotAffectDevice` pins CNA's current (deliberate) value-copy behavior for `BlendState`; `DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState`/`SamplerState` are presumed to share the identical pattern (same value-member shape in `GraphicsDevice.hpp`) but not independently pinned by a test yet. Fix shape, if ever pursued: would require every affected `GraphicsDevice` state property to become a reference/pointer type (e.g. `std::shared_ptr<BlendState>`) project-wide — a real architecture decision with ripple effects across all 4 state classes and their call sites, not a small patch. One deliberate upside already noted (Tasks 291/301): because CNA's static presets (`BlendState::Opaque` etc.) are C++ `static const`, they structurally cannot be accidentally mutated the way FNA's mutable `static readonly` reference-type presets technically can (`BlendState.Opaque.AlphaBlendFunction = ...;` compiles in C# and corrupts the shared static instance app-wide) — so this value-semantics deviation trades away one XNA/FNA behavior in exchange for eliminating a different, real XNA/FNA footgun. Not fixed here — needs its own dedicated architecture task, and only if a concrete game use case ever needs it. |
| 870 | Implement real `DepthBufferFunction` mapping and full stencil-test support on Vulkan (Task 313 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED — real per-DepthBufferFunction pipeline selection and full front/back stencil-op support now implemented on Vulkan, fixing 6 of the 12 pre-existing Vulkan failures this bug caused (`CompareFunction`, `StencilEnable`, `StencilMask`, `StencilOps`, `StencilTwoSided`, `GraphicsDevice.ReferenceStencil`).** `VulkanGraphicsBackend::ApplyDepthStencilState` now stores all 15 previously-dropped parameters into a new `DepthStencilKeyParams` struct (shared with the pipeline-creation layer) instead of only `depthTestEnabled_`/`depthWriteEnabled_`. New `ToVkCompareOp`/`ToVkStencilOp` helpers map XNA's `CompareFunction`/`StencilOperation` ordinals to `VkCompareOp`/`VkStencilOp` exactly mirroring EasyGL's already-correct `ToEasyGLCompareFunc`/`ToEasyGLStencilOp`. A new shared `FillDepthStencilState()` fills a pipeline's `depthCompareOp` and front/back `VkStencilOpState` from `DepthStencilKeyParams`, replacing every one of the 9 (not 7 — Task 899 added 2 more since this row was written) Vulkan 3D pipeline-creation functions' previously-hardcoded `VK_COMPARE_OP_LESS`/`VK_COMPARE_OP_LESS_OR_EQUAL` split. Every pipeline's cache key (`Make3DKey`/`MakeExt3DKey`) now folds in a new 29-bit `PackDepthStencilBits()` block (depthFunc + full front/CCW stencil func/fail/depthFail/pass + twoSidedStencilMode), widening `Make3DKey`'s return type from `uint32_t` to `uint64_t` (and `pipelines3D_`'s map key type to match) since the combined key no longer fits 32 bits. Stencil reference/compare-mask/write-mask are real Vulkan **dynamic** state (`VK_DYNAMIC_STATE_STENCIL_REFERENCE`/`COMPARE_MASK`/`WRITE_MASK`, added to every 3D pipeline's dynamic-state list) rather than baked into the pipeline — `vkCmdSetStencilReference`/`CompareMask`/`WriteMask` are now called once per draw in `draw3DFor`, mirroring the existing `vkCmdSetDepthBias`-every-draw convention, sourced from 3 new `Pending3DDraw` fields populated at all 5 draw-call sites. `FindDepthFormat()`'s candidate order was reversed to prefer stencil-capable formats first (`VK_FORMAT_D24_UNORM_S8_UINT`/`VK_FORMAT_D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT`) over `VK_FORMAT_D32_SFLOAT` — previously `D32_SFLOAT` (mandatory-support, stencil-less) was checked first and almost always won, meaning the chosen depth/stencil buffer almost never actually had a stencil aspect regardless of how correctly `ApplyDepthStencilState` mapped the request. **A second, separate, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed as a hard prerequisite for `Vulkan_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`**: `GraphicsDevice::setReferenceStencilProperty()` only ever updated its own local field, never forwarding to any backend at all — a gap shared by EasyGL and Bgfx too (confirmed via grep: neither backend caches `referenceStencil` as a re-appliable member, both only consume it as an immediate `ApplyDepthStencilState` parameter). Added a new `IGraphicsBackend::SetReferenceStencil(int)` virtual (default no-op, mirroring the already-established `SetBlendFactor` pattern for exactly this kind of standalone device-level property) and a real Vulkan override; `GraphicsDevice::setReferenceStencilProperty()` now calls it. EasyGL/Bgfx's equivalent gap is a real, separate, already-failing bug (`EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil` was independently reconfirmed pre-existing during Task 902's regression work this same session) — deliberately not fixed here, since it needs backend-specific state-caching work outside this task's Vulkan-only scope; left open for a future task. **A genuinely surprising empirical finding while fixing `StencilTwoSided`**: with `rs.frontFace = VK_FRONT_FACE_CLOCKWISE` and this backend's vertex shaders' `pos.y = -pos.y` Y-flip, `VkPipelineDepthStencilStateCreateInfo::front`/`back` had to be assigned the OPPOSITE of the initially-expected winding to produce correct results — confirmed via a genuinely differential two-column test (one column's back-facing triangle must apply `CounterClockwiseStencilFunction`/`Fail`, the other must not) failing until front/back were swapped. Root cause not fully isolated (this project's existing culling tests, which use the identical `frontFace` value, are unaffected and already correct, so this is plausibly an llvmpipe/Mesa software-rasterizer-specific quirk in its own front/back `VkStencilOpState` assignment rather than a general CNA winding error) — documented in code as an empirically-verified, deliberately pragmatic swap rather than a fully-explained fix. **A real, necessary test update**: `vulkan_depth_bias_test.cpp` relied on the OLD, buggy hardcoded `VK_COMPARE_OP_LESS` behavior of the plain `GetOrCreatePipeline3D`/`GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D` pipelines to make its "redraw at equal depth" trick discriminate anything — under XNA's REAL default `DepthStencilState.DepthBufferFunction=LessEqual` (confirmed in `DepthStencilState.cpp`), an equal-depth redraw always passes regardless of bias, so this test's premise cannot work against the correct default at all. Fixed by having the test explicitly request `CompareFunction::Less` via a real `DepthStencilState`, matching what its own header comment already claimed was happening. **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash`**: reverting all 5 production/test files reproduced the exact predicted failures on all 6 previously-broken tests (`CompareFunction`, `StencilEnable`, `StencilMask`, `StencilOps`, `StencilTwoSided`, `GraphicsDevice.ReferenceStencil` — 0/6 passed, matching this row's own 5x-reconfirmed pre-fix diagnosis exactly); restored and reconfirmed all 6 pass. **Full Vulkan regression**: `ctest` 4368/4377 (9 pre-existing: 5 already-tracked Task 868 `BlendState` failures — a separate, unrelated bug — + 1 already-documented `Vulkan_DepthBias` sub-case + 3 already-confirmed-pre-existing `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` segfaults, all reproduced identically via the same `git stash`), `CnaTests` (filtered) 4278/4280 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match). Task 870 closes 6 of the original 12 pre-existing Vulkan `ctest` failures this project has carried since Task 896; the remaining 6 are unrelated, already-tracked/documented issues. |
| 871 | `GraphicsDevice::Clear` ignores `ClearOptions::Stencil` and the `stencil` clear value entirely, on every backend (Task 315 finding) | ⬜ | `GraphicsDevice::Clear(ClearOptions options, const Color& color, float depth, int stencil)` explicitly discards its `stencil` parameter (`(void)stencil;`) and never checks `hasClearFlag(options, ClearOptions::Stencil)` — confirmed via reading the full function body, there is no code path that clears the stencil buffer to any value, requested or not. This is a real divergence from FNA, where `GraphicsDevice.Clear(Color)` (the single-arg overload) actually clears `Target \| DepthBuffer \| Stencil` together, and the full overload threads `stencil` through to `FNA3D_Clear` correctly. Confirmed on EasyGL specifically: none of `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::Clear`/`ClearColorAndDepth`/`ClearDepth` ever pass `::easygl::ClearFlags::Stencil` to the underlying `device.clear(...)` call. On Vulkan, the effect is masked by a separate quirk: `stencilLoadOp = VK_ATTACHMENT_LOAD_OP_DONT_CARE` on every render pass means the stencil aspect's initial content is undefined at the start of each render pass regardless of what `Clear()` does — so the two bugs compound rather than one hiding the other. Found while designing Task 315's `StencilEnable` pixel test: had to work around this by explicitly "stamping" a known stencil value via a real draw call instead of relying on `GraphicsDevice::Clear(ClearOptions::Stencil, ...)`, which would otherwise silently do nothing. Fix shape: add stencil clearing to `GraphicsDevice::Clear` (threading the requested `stencil` value into each backend's `Clear`/`ClearColorAndDepth`/`ClearDepth`-equivalent calls, likely needs new `ClearColorDepthAndStencil`/`ClearStencil` backend methods mirroring the existing depth ones) and set Vulkan's `stencilLoadOp` appropriately (`VK_ATTACHMENT_LOAD_OP_CLEAR` when a stencil clear is requested, `LOAD` otherwise, matching the existing depth `loadOp` handling). Not fixed here — small in principle but touches all 3 backends' `Clear`-family methods; needs its own dedicated task with a real pixel/unit test proving the stencil clear value round-trips correctly, not bundled into Phase 37's per-property verification tasks. |
| 872 | Implement `GraphicsDevice.ReferenceStencil` as a real, independent, backend-connected override on all 3 backends (Task 319 finding) | ⬜ | **Status corrected (2026-07-09) — this row's own original diagnosis was already stale, found while investigating Task 838/871 in the Phase 72/73 follow-up.** `IGraphicsBackend::SetReferenceStencil` DOES now exist and IS implemented on Vulkan (`VulkanGraphicsBackend::SetReferenceStencil`, `referenceStencil_ = value`, threaded per-deferred-draw into `Pending3DDraw::referenceStencil` and applied via a real `vkCmdSetStencilReference` dynamic-state call before each 3D draw) — this was added as an undocumented side effect of Task 870's own stencil dynamic-state overhaul, never folded back into this row. **Confirmed via direct empirical re-run**: `cna_test_vulkan_graphicsdevice_reference_stencil` (the exact same discriminating test described below) genuinely PASSES on current Vulkan, both in isolation and as part of the full 4444/4448 Task 925 regression (not in the failure list). **EasyGL and Bgfx remain genuinely broken** — confirmed via grep, neither backend overrides `SetReferenceStencil` at all (falls to the shared no-op default); `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil` is still failing in the current, live test baseline (Task 924's own regression, 2026-07-09). Real remaining scope is now 2 backends, not 3. Original diagnosis preserved below for the EasyGL-relevant parts (still accurate for that backend): `GraphicsDevice::setReferenceStencilProperty(int value)` **on EasyGL/Bgfx** is a pure local field write with zero backend connection. Confirmed via FNA source (`GraphicsDevice.cs`) that `GraphicsDevice.ReferenceStencil` is meant to be a real, independent device property (`FNA3D_Get/SetReferenceStencil`), settable without reassigning the whole `DepthStencilState` and expected to immediately affect subsequent stencil compares — FNA's own source even has a `FIXME: Does this affect the value found in DepthStencilState?` comment confirming this is intentionally an override, not a read-through proxy. `examples/easygl_graphicsdevice_reference_stencil_test.cpp` proves the gap empirically on EasyGL (Vulkan's own copy of this same test now passes, see above): stamp stencil=0x05, assign a compare `DepthStencilState` with a baked-in `ReferenceStencil=0x05` (would PASS at face value under `StencilFunction::Equal`), then call `GraphicsDevice::setReferenceStencilProperty(0x99)` directly (not via a new state) and draw — a correct implementation must REJECT (0x99≠0x05); EasyGL/Bgfx still incorrectly PASS (override never reaches the GPU, the state's own stale baked-in 0x05 is still what gets compared), Vulkan now correctly REJECTS (fixed, see the status-correction note above). (`GraphicsDevice::setDepthStencilStateProperty` DOES correctly propagate a *newly-assigned* state's own `ReferenceStencil` into `GraphicsDevice`'s local `referenceStencil_` field as of Task 319's fix — that part works; this bug is specifically about changing the reference *independently*, without reassigning the whole state, which XNA/FNA supports and this project currently cannot.) Fix shape: add a `SetReferenceStencil(int value)` method to `IGraphicsBackend`; EasyGL needs to cache the currently-active `StencilFunction`/masks (not currently stored as members, only passed straight through to `glStencilFunc`/`glStencilFuncSeparate` per `ApplyDepthStencilState` call) so it can reissue the same compare with just the new reference; Vulkan is now done (see the status-correction note above — `vkCmdSetStencilReference` genuinely wired in as dynamic pipeline state, a side effect of Task 870); Bgfx needs to patch just the reference bits of its already-packed `stencilFront_`/`stencilBack_` state via `BGFX_STENCIL_FUNC_REF_MASK`/`BuildBgfxStencil`-style helpers without requiring a full `ApplyDepthStencilState` call. Not fixed here for EasyGL/Bgfx — needs its own dedicated task and full regression pass across those 2 backends, matching Task 868/870/871's precedent of tracking-not-fixing large multi-backend gaps found during a verification task. |
| 873 | Fix `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw`'s wrong-handle-type cast when sampling a `RenderTarget2D` (Task 333 finding) | ✅ | **Fixed as a hard prerequisite of Task 878/879's Bgfx MSAA test** (its RT-then-backbuffer-sample methodology cannot produce a meaningful result without this fix). Implemented exactly the fix shape this row's own writeup predicted: a new `IBgfxSamplable` interface (`virtual bgfx::TextureHandle GetBgfxTextureHandle() const`) implemented by both `BgfxTextureBackend` (`return textureHandle;`) and `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` (`return colorTex;`), with `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` now `dynamic_cast`-ing to it instead of blindly `static_cast`-ing to `BgfxTextureBackend`; `BgfxGraphicsBackend::SubmitSprite`'s signature changed from taking a `const BgfxTextureBackend&` to a raw `bgfx::TextureHandle` + width/height so it no longer assumes a concrete type either. Scoped only to the `SpriteBatch::Draw` path this task actually needed — the ~8 other unsafe `static_cast<const BgfxTextureBackend&>` sites in `DrawPrimitivesEx`'s stock-effect texture binding (unrelated, much larger surface area) were deliberately left untouched. **Real, severe, previously-only-suspected bug, now confirmed.** `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` (`BgfxGraphicsBackend.cpp`) unconditionally does `static_cast<const BgfxTextureBackend&>(texture)` on whatever `ITextureBackend` it receives. `RenderTarget2D`'s backend is a `BgfxRenderTargetBackend`, which inherits `IRenderTargetBackend`/`ITextureBackend` directly — **not** `BgfxTextureBackend` (an unrelated sibling class) — so this is a `static_cast` between unrelated concrete types, sharing only a common interface ancestor. Because `bgfx::FrameBufferHandle` and `bgfx::TextureHandle` are both `struct { uint16_t idx; }` (identical layout, confirmed in `bgfx.h`'s `BGFX_HANDLE` macro) and both are the first data member of their respective classes, the cast compiles and doesn't crash, but reads `BgfxRenderTargetBackend::fbo` (the framebuffer handle) where `BgfxTextureBackend::textureHandle` would be — i.e. it hands bgfx a **framebuffer-pool handle index where a texture-pool handle index is expected**, sampling whatever unrelated texture (or nothing) happens to occupy that index in bgfx's separate texture-handle pool, not the RT's actual `colorTex` color attachment. Confirmed via direct layout analysis (deterministic, not speculative) and via a new runtime smoke test, `examples/bgfx_render_target_sample_test.cpp` (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_SampleAfterUnbind`), which exercises exactly this path — it does not crash (bgfx has no strong handle-type validation to catch this), consistent with silently sampling the wrong data rather than throwing. This upgrades Task 179's informal suspicion ("SpriteBatch casts textures to BgfxTextureBackend, not BgfxRenderTargetBackend") into a confirmed, precisely-diagnosed bug. Cannot be pixel-verified — Bgfx has no usable GPU pixel-readback path in this project (established precedent: Tasks 179/249/278). Fix shape: give `ITextureBackend` (or a Bgfx-specific extension point) a virtual accessor for "the `bgfx::TextureHandle` to sample", implemented correctly by both `BgfxTextureBackend` (`return textureHandle;`) and `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` (`return colorTex;`), and have `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` (and any other Bgfx code path that samples an arbitrary `ITextureBackend`, e.g. stock-effect texture binding) call that accessor instead of blindly `static_cast`-ing to `BgfxTextureBackend`. Verification without pixel readback: assert the extracted handle's `.idx` equals `BgfxRenderTargetBackend::colorTex.idx`, not `fbo.idx`, after the fix. Contained to the Bgfx backend only (`BgfxGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp`) — does not touch the XNA API surface or other backends. Not fixed here — needs its own dedicated task per this project's discipline of not bundling backend-internal fixes into a verify-only audit task, and because no pixel test can confirm the fix visually, only structurally. |
| 874 | Fix Bgfx's wrong-handle-type cast when sampling a `RenderTargetCube` via `EnvironmentMapEffect` (Task 334 finding) | ✅ | **Backlog-hygiene closure (2026-07-09) — no new work, already fixed under a later task number, left unmarked.** Fixed by Task 907 as a verification prerequisite: added the exact `IBgfxCubeSamplable` interface (mirroring `IBgfxSamplable`, Task 873's own fix shape) this row's own diagnosis predicted, implemented by both `BgfxTextureCubeBackend`/`BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend`, dispatched via a safe `dynamic_cast` instead of the blind `static_cast`. See `plan_graphics.md` Task 907 for full detail. **Same bug shape as Task 873, one level over — confirmed by direct layout analysis.** `BgfxGraphicsBackend`'s `envMapping` draw branch does `static_cast<const BgfxTextureCubeBackend&>(*params.envMap)`. A `RenderTargetCube`'s backend is a `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend`, which inherits `IRenderTargetCubeBackend`/`ITextureCubeBackend` — **not** `BgfxTextureCubeBackend` (an unrelated sibling class). `BgfxTextureCubeBackend`'s first data member is `bgfx::TextureHandle handle`; `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend`'s first data member is `bgfx::FrameBufferHandle fbo` (its actual colour texture, `cubeTex`, is the SECOND member) — both handle structs are `struct { uint16_t idx; }` (same `BGFX_HANDLE` macro as Task 873), so the cast compiles and doesn't crash, but reads `fbo` where `handle` should be: a framebuffer-pool handle passed where a texture-pool handle is expected. Confirmed via a new runtime smoke test, `examples/bgfx_render_target_cube_sample_test.cpp` (`Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`) — no crash, consistent with silently sampling wrong data. Fix shape: identical to Task 873's — give `ITextureCubeBackend` a virtual accessor for "the `bgfx::TextureHandle` to sample", implemented by `BgfxTextureCubeBackend` (`return handle;`) and `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` (`return cubeTex;`), and use it in the `envMapping` branch instead of the blind `static_cast`. Likely worth fixing together with Task 873 in the same pass, since both need the identical accessor pattern on the 2D and cube interfaces respectively. Not fixed here — same reasoning as Task 873 (own dedicated task, no pixel-verifiable fix confirmation on Bgfx). |
| 875 | Fix Vulkan: `Clear()` with no draw call into a bound render target never records a render pass (Task 334 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, confirmed bug fixed per this row's own predicted fix shape (a).** Added a new `std::vector<VulkanRTSource*> clearedRTs_` member to `VulkanGraphicsBackend`: `Clear()`/`ClearColorAndDepth()` now push `currentRT_` onto it (when a render target is bound, deduplicated) in addition to their existing clear-colour-scalar bookkeeping; `RecordCommandBuffer`'s `usedRTs` construction now seeds from `clearedRTs_` before scanning `activeBatches_`/`pending3D_` (its previous, draw-call-only sources); `clearedRTs_` is cleared alongside `activeBatches_`/`pending3D_` once per frame. **A key nuance discovered while designing the regression test**: `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget()`'s own existing `RenderTargetUsage::DiscardContents`-triggers-an-implicit-clear behavior (the default usage) dispatches through `ClearColorAndDepth()`, not `Clear()` — meaning `ClearColorAndDepth()`'s copy of this fix is actually the one doing the real work for the common case; `Clear()`'s own copy only matters for `RenderTargetUsage::PreserveContents` targets or a `ClearOptions::Target`-only clear. Both were fixed identically since both needed it regardless. New `Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_ClearOnlyRoundtrip` test (`vulkan_rt_roundtrip_test.cpp`, port of `easygl_rt_roundtrip_test.cpp`/Task 180): fills 2 separate `RenderTarget2D`s via `Clear()`-only (no draw call) then samples each back via `SpriteBatch` after unbinding (the same proven-good RT-as-texture path Task 148 already uses) — **deliberately does NOT read the RT directly while still bound**, unlike the EasyGL original it ports from; investigation confirmed Vulkan's `GetBackBufferData`/`ReadBackbuffer` unconditionally reads the swapchain image regardless of whatever RT is currently bound (unlike EasyGL, which tracks `currentRtHeight_` and redirects) — an initial direct port that tried reading "while bound" only appeared to pass by coincidence (the swapchain's own backbuffer clear shares the same global clear-colour scalar as whichever RT was most recently `Clear()`-ed in the same recorded frame), and was caught failing at its 3rd, more revealing assertion rather than accepted as a false 2/3 pass — the same discriminating-power discipline this project applies throughout. The sample-after-unbind redesign also needed one more fix: forcing a frame boundary (dummy `GetBackBufferData` read) immediately after each RT's Clear-only fill, since Vulkan has no per-RT-remembered clear value — the shared global clear-colour scalar would otherwise be overwritten by the backbuffer's own subsequent `Clear()` call before the RT's render pass actually recorded, corrupting its content. **Discriminating power independently verified**: temporarily disabled both `clearedRTs_.push_back()` call sites (matching the true pre-fix state, not just one of the two) and reran — reproduced pure black `(0,0,0)` on both RTs instead of their fill colours; restored and reconfirmed 2/2 PASS. Full regression: `CnaTests` (Vulkan, filtered) 4262/4264 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); targeted `ctest -R "RenderTarget\|Mip\|Msaa\|MultiSample"` 64/64 (was 63/63 before this task's new test). |
| 876 | Investigate why sampling a `RenderTargetCube` via `EnvironmentMapEffect` renders black instead of its actual rendered content on Vulkan (Task 334 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED, verify-only — the bug no longer reproduces.** Rebuilt and reran `examples/vulkan_rendertargetcube_sample_test.cpp` (`Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`) unmodified while investigating this task: it now **passes cleanly and consistently** (`centre=(0,0,255)`, exact expected blue — confirmed across 5 repeated runs plus a dedicated `ctest -R` invocation, no flakiness observed). This is the SAME test/methodology that originally found the bug (real `SpriteBatch` draws into all 6 `RenderTargetCube` faces, unbind, sample via `EnvironmentMapEffect`) — not a weakened or false-positive reproduction. Root cause not retroactively bisected to an exact prior commit (would require a from-scratch vendored-submodule build environment at an older checkout, not worth the cost once the bug is confirmed gone) — most likely an incidental side effect of this session's substantial `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend` rework for Task 907 (full-mip-range `cubeView_`, construction-time full-range initial layout transitions, per-face `FaceProxy` changes), though Task 907's own new initial-transition fix was gated on `levelCount_ > 1` and this test uses `mipMap=false` (`levelCount_ == 1`), so that specific fix is not obviously the direct cause either — genuinely unclear which change fixed it, only that something did.
