| 732 | Implement real `SurfaceFormat` GPU forwarding on EasyGL | ⛔ | **Real architecture decision needed — see question below.** Investigated the actual current state before writing any code, per this project's own blast-radius-check discipline. Confirmed the task's own premise (`CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` hardcode `::metagl::InternalFormat::Rgba8` regardless of the `surfaceFormat` parameter — `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp`'s `EasyGLTexture3DBackend::EasyGLTexture3DBackend(int, int, int, bool, int /*surfaceFormat*/)` and `EasyGLTextureCubeBackend::EasyGLTextureCubeBackend(int, bool, int /*surfaceFormat*/)` both literally comment out the parameter name) — but this dead code is currently **unreachable from any live call path**: the shared, cross-backend `Texture::ValidateFormat(SurfaceFormat)` (`Texture.cpp:109`), called unconditionally by every format-taking `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` constructor on ALL FOUR backends, already throws `std::runtime_error` for every `SurfaceFormat` value except `Color` — so none of the 14 formats this task names can ever reach the EasyGL backend's `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` today, regardless of backend. This is not an oversight: it is an existing, ALREADY-SHIPPED, ALREADY-TESTED contract from Task 176 (`examples/easygl_surface_format_throws_test.cpp`), which explicitly asserts `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` constructed with `Bgr565`/`HalfVector4`/`HdrBlendable`/`Vector4`/etc. (multiple of the EXACT formats this task names) **must throw** — and is separately, explicitly documented as an accepted, by-design, cross-backend limitation in `NEXT.md` §5 ("`SurfaceFormat` support is Color-only for real GPU formats"). Implementing this task as literally scoped would directly reverse that shipped decision (a regression against Task 176's own test) via a change to SHARED code (`Texture::ValidateFormat`) affecting ALL FOUR backends, not an EasyGL-only fix, despite being filed under an EasyGL-only phase. **Deeper blast radius, found while investigating further**: even if `ValidateFormat` were loosened for these 14 formats, CNA's ENTIRE public `SetData`/`GetData` API surface for `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` is exclusively `Color*`-based (4 bytes/pixel RGBA) — there is no way for any caller to supply correctly-packed non-RGBA8 data (e.g. a 2-byte `Bgr565` texel, a 16-byte float `Vector4` texel) at all today. `Texture3D` alone has one `NOXNA SetDataPointerEXT(..., const void* data, int dataLength)` raw-bytes escape hatch; `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` have none (`Texture2D::SetDataRGBA` is RGBA8-only, not format-agnostic). Genuinely implementing "real GPU forwarding" for these 14 formats therefore needs, at minimum: (a) loosening the shared `ValidateFormat` for exactly these 14 values (reversing Task 176's contract, cross-backend); (b) adding/extending raw-or-typed `SetData`/`GetData` overloads for `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` (mirroring `Texture3D::SetDataPointerEXT`'s precedent) so a caller can actually supply/read non-RGBA8 pixel data; (c) correct bytes-per-pixel math throughout mip-level-size/`GetData`-buffer-size calculations (currently hardcoded to 4 everywhere); (d) the EasyGL-side format-mapping table this task's own note focuses on. (a)-(c) are cross-cutting API/architecture decisions well beyond "map an enum to GL internal formats in one backend file." **Question for the project owner:** (a) do the full job — extend the public `SetData`/`GetData` API surface (a real, generic-or-typed data path, not just `Color*`), loosen `ValidateFormat` for these 14 formats across all 4 backends, then implement EasyGL's GLES 3.2 format table (large, multi-file, cross-backend architecture change, arguably deserving its own dedicated multi-task plan rather than one line item); or (b) implement GPU-allocation-only forwarding — have `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` (and `CreateTexture`, if given format info) allocate the GPU texture with the correct internal format for these 14 values, verified via a backend-level test that bypasses the public `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` API (since the public API still can't supply/read the data) — narrower, but produces plumbing the public API can't use yet, and still requires deciding whether `ValidateFormat` moves at all; or (c) leave the current, already-documented, already-tested "Color-only" limitation as-is, and treat this task as superseded/closed-won't-do unless a future, properly-scoped architecture task (generic `SetData<T>`/raw-bytes API design, spanning all 4 backends) is planned separately. Not guessing on this per project's own risky-decision policy — leaving BLOCKED until the project owner picks a direction. Zero code changes made — this was investigation only. |
| 733 | Implement `FillMode::WireFrame` emulation on EasyGL via barycentric-coordinate geometry shader + `fwidth()` edge detection | ✅ | **CLOSED — already implemented, already tested; the task's own premise was stale.** Investigated before writing any code (per this project's own verify-first discipline). Confirmed `FillMode::WireFrame` is ALREADY emulated on EasyGL — not via a geometry shader, but via a simpler, already-shipped technique: `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::ApplyRasterizerState` sets a `wireframe_` flag when `fillMode==1`, and every 3D draw entry point (`DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawTexturedPrimitives`/etc.) calls `DrawWireframe()` first when that flag is set, which re-expands the triangle list/strip's index stream into a scratch `GL_LINES` element buffer (one edge-pair per triangle side) and issues a `GL_LINES` draw instead of `GL_TRIANGLES` — correctly handling both `TriangleList`/`TriangleStrip`, indexed and non-indexed, 16-bit and 32-bit indices, and `baseVertex`. This is real, complete, working code (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp`), not a stub — the task's own "N/A on GLES" framing being corrected was already out of date. **Also already has test coverage**: `EasyGL_FillMode_Solid` (registered in `CMakeLists.txt`, reusing `vulkan_fill_mode_test.cpp` verbatim per this project's own established backend-agnostic-test-reuse convention, e.g. Task 326) already exercises exactly this — a real triangle drawn Solid (centre must be RED), then WireFrame (centre must be BLACK, interior not rasterized), then reset to Solid (centre RED again) — and already passes 3/3 on a fresh run this session (confirmed directly, not assumed from a stale log). The test's own name is a slight misnomer (it validates all 3 sub-tests, not just Solid) but its actual assertions are exactly what this task needs. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaged `ApplyRasterizerState` to force `wireframe_ = false` unconditionally — rebuilt and reran, and exactly the WireFrame sub-test failed as predicted (centre read RED instead of BLACK, 2/3 pass) while the two Solid sub-tests stayed correct; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed 3/3 pass. **Why the proposed barycentric-coordinate/geometry-shader technique was NOT implemented instead**: replacing a working, correct, already-tested implementation with a fundamentally different, more complex one for no behavioral gain would violate this project's own "don't add complexity beyond what's required" and "match XNA/FNA behavior over personal C++ preference" conventions. Real hardware `glPolygonMode(GL_LINE)` (what FNA/XNA's `FillMode::WireFrame` actually models) produces thin, non-anti-aliased edge lines — exactly what the existing `GL_LINES` re-expansion already produces — whereas a `fwidth()`-based barycentric-distance technique is the standard idiom for a THICK, anti-aliased stylized wireframe-overlay effect, a deliberately different, non-XNA-faithful visual. That technique remains a viable FUTURE option if a stylized overlay effect is ever explicitly wanted, but implementing it now would be speculative, unrequested scope, not a fix for any real gap. Wrote and then deleted a duplicate EasyGL-specific test file after discovering the existing reused-Vulkan-source test already covers this fully (per this project's reuse-over-duplication convention) — zero net files added. No code changes shipped (the sabotage-and-revert touched already-shipped code with zero net diff); no separate `ctest` regression needed since no production code changed net. |

Task 733 investigated first and found `FillMode::WireFrame` is ALREADY correctly emulated on EasyGL via a completely different, simpler, already-shipped technique (`DrawWireframe`'s `GL_LINES` index re-expansion) that involves no geometry shader at all — and, per Task 733's own write-up, implementing the geometry-shader/barycentric approach was explicitly declined as unrequested scope replacing a working, XNA-faithful implementation. With no geometry-shader-based WireFrame implementation in existence (by design, not by omission), there is nothing for this task's "non-geometry-shader fallback" to fall back FROM — the existing `GL_LINES` technique already IS the universally-portable, driver-independent, no-geometry-shader-required implementation this task was trying to provide as a fallback. No code changes needed; no `docs/easygl_bugs.md` note needed since there is no unreliable-geometry-shader-driver risk to document (no geometry shader is used for wireframe at all). |

CalculateTexture3DMipLevels(w, h) : 1;`, followed by a per-level `set_image_3d(...)` loop that pre-allocates real GPU storage for every mip level, halving width/height/depth each level (standard volume-mip behavior; depth doesn't participate in the level *count*, matching FNA's `Texture3D` constructor and `Texture3D.cpp`'s own `CalculateMipLevels(w,h)`, but each level's own storage still shrinks in all 3 dimensions). CalculateCubeMipLevels(size) : 1;`, followed by a nested per-face/per-level `set_image_2d(...)` loop that pre-allocates real GPU storage for every mip level of all 6 cube faces, halving `levelSize` each level (cube faces are square, matching `TextureCube.cpp`'s own `CalculateMipLevels(size,size)`). | 737 | Add explicit `TextureAddressMode::Mirror` regression test for the Task 269 SpriteBatch SamplerState fix | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, previously-unverified gap in test coverage closed; no production bug found (the underlying fix already covers Mirror correctly).** Task 269 fixed `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s `SamplerState` having no effect on EasyGL by adding `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode` overrides, and its own test (`easygl_texture_address_mode_test.cpp`) independently pixel-verified `Wrap` and `Clamp` at `U=1.25` — but never `Mirror`, even though `Mirror` is threaded through the exact same `pendingAddressU_`/`pendingAddressV_` fields and the exact same `SetSamplerAddressMode` call. Distinct from Task 296's existing `easygl_texture_address_mode_mirror_effect_test.cpp`, which verifies `Mirror` via `DrawUserPrimitives`+`DualTextureEffect` and `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0]` directly — a completely different code path that never touches `ISpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerAddressMode` at all. FNA has no `PointMirror` static preset (only `*Clamp`/`*Wrap` for `Point`/`Linear`/`Anisotropic`), so the new test builds a custom `SamplerState` (`Filter=Point`, `AddressU=AddressV=Mirror`), mirroring Task 296's own approach. Reused Task 269's exact methodology (2×1 Red\|Blue texture, `SpriteBatch::Draw` with a `sourceRectangle` 2× the texture width so sampled `U` spans `[0,2]`) but sampled at `U=1.6` instead of Task 269's `U=1.25` — deliberately, since `Mirror` and `Clamp` coincidentally agree at `U=1.25` (both land on the texture's last texel), which would make the check non-discriminating; at `U=1.6`, `Wrap`→fract(1.6)=0.6→Blue, `Clamp`→clamps to last texel→Blue, `Mirror`→reflects 2.0-1.6=0.4→Red — all three modes cleanly separated (matches Task 296's own `U=1.6` choice for the identical reason). New `examples/easygl_texture_address_mode_mirror_test.cpp`, registered as `EasyGL_TextureAddressMode_Mirror`: asserts `Mirror`→Red as the actual subject, and additionally asserts both `Wrap`/`Clamp`→Blue at the same sample point purely to confirm the point genuinely discriminates (not part of the pass/fail gate's headline claim, but still checked, unlike Task 685's SDL_Renderer test which only prints its context checks). Passes on the first attempt with the exact predicted RGB values. **Discriminating power independently verified via targeted sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily reduced `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerAddressMode` to a no-op (simulating Task 269's pre-fix silent-no-op state) — rebuilt and reran, and the test failed exactly as predicted (`Mirror` sample read Blue instead of Red, the same wrong answer a broken/reverted fix would produce); restored the two-line assignment (confirmed clean `git diff` — zero net production-code change from this task) and reconfirmed pass. ## Phase 72 — Bgfx: full 2D+3D pixel-verified parity

> Runs third. Unlike the EasyGL phase above, this one is genuinely large — Bgfx is not the
> default target of any existing Phase 34–55 task, so the full breadth of pixel-verification
> work needs an explicit, separate pass. Starts from wiring up the pixel-readback path itself,
> since no current Bgfx test uses it at all.
>
> **Full row-by-row triage completed 2026-07-09** (the first ever full pass — Task 861 had only
> spot-checked a sample of Phase 73, and this phase had never been triaged at all): of 85 rows,
> 34 were SUPERSEDED by later work and closed here as a backlog-hygiene pass, 38 were confirmed
> REAL GAPS. **Tasks 752-755/758-768/803 (15 of those 38, the entire `DepthStencilState` row group,
> all 3 testable `RasterizerState` rows, all 4 `BlendState` preset rows, and the first `SpriteBatch`
> row) were picked up as real work the same session** — **Task 803's own finding is arguably the
> single most impactful bug found in this entire pass**: `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s final overload
> never applied its `depthStencilState` parameter at all (silently discarded, matching this
> project's now-familiar "commented-out unused parameter" shape), so SpriteBatch draws inherited
> whatever `DepthStencilState` a game's 3D rendering last configured instead of FNA's real default
> of `DepthStencilState.None` — a bug in **shared code affecting all 4 backends**, previously
> masked on EasyGL/Vulkan purely because OpenGL's own hardware default happens to have depth
> testing disabled (coincidence, not correctness), while Bgfx's own non-trivial `depthFlags_`
> default exposed it for the first time. Fixed in `SpriteBatch.cpp` directly; verified with a full
> regression across all 4 backends (Bgfx/EasyGL/Vulkan/SDL_Renderer), zero new failures anywhere.
> The first-ever Bgfx `DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState` pixel tests
> found 6 more genuine, confirmed, previously-
> undiscovered bugs/gaps: `DepthBufferWriteEnable=false` had zero effect on any 3D draw (all 4
> draw-dispatch functions unconditionally wrote depth regardless), fixed at Task 758/759;
> `TwoSidedStencilMode`'s front/back stencil slots were swapped relative to the intended raw
> winding (Task 763) — the exact same phenomenon already found and fixed for Vulkan at Task 870,
> previously undetected on Bgfx purely for lack of a differential two-sided-stencil pixel test;
> `GraphicsDevice.ReferenceStencil`'s standalone setter was a silent no-op on Bgfx (Task 764,
> mirroring this project's own prior finding that only Vulkan had a real `SetReferenceStencil`
> override); `FillMode::WireFrame` had no implementation at all on Bgfx (Task 766, a genuine

### Foundational: unlock pixel testing

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 740 | Wire `GraphicsDevice::GetBackBufferData` into a reusable Bgfx pixel-readback test helper | ✅ | **Backlog-hygiene closure (2026-07-09) — SUPERSEDED, no new work, left unmarked (found via a full Phase 72/73 triage pass).** Covered by real GPU pixel readback via `GetBackBufferData` is wired and used pervasively across 57+ `cna_bgfx_test` registrations; Task 406 established the retry-until-non-black convention.  The async `bgfx::requestScreenShot`+`bgfx::frame()` path exists (Task 117, done) but zero current tests call it — this unblocks every pixel test below |
| 741 | Add a Bgfx per-texture-level readback path for direct `Texture2D`/`3D`/`Cube` `GetData` verification | ✅ | **2026-07-10 — SUPERSEDED, already fully covered by earlier work, no new code needed.** `Texture2D` doesn't need a GPU readback path at all — confirmed via source (Task 817's own investigation) that `Texture2D::SetData`/`GetData` operate entirely on a backend-agnostic CPU-side `cpuPixels_` shadow buffer, never touching the GPU on the read side. `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` already got a real per-texture-level GPU readback path from **Task 914** (`BgfxTexture3DBackend`/`BgfxTextureCubeBackend::GetData`: blit into a temporary `BGFX_TEXTURE_BLIT_DST`\|`READ_BACK` texture, then `bgfx::readTexture()`), already exercised by `Bgfx_Texture3D_Mip_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_TextureCube_Mip_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_TextureCube_PartialRect_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_Texture3D_PartialBox_Readback` (Task 819, this session) — none of which need a full draw+backbuffer-read cycle, exactly this row's own stated goal. |
| 742 | Verify Bgfx `GetBackBufferData` readback reliability under headless CI | ✅ | **2026-07-10 — verified via extensive existing empirical evidence, no new dedicated test needed.** The "up to 3 frames" wait this row references (`BgfxGraphicsBackend::ReadBackbuffer`, `for (int attempt = 0; attempt < 3 && !readbackCallback_.screenshotReady; ++attempt) bgfx::frame();`, throws `std::runtime_error` if still not ready) has been exercised many thousands of times across this session alone: every one of the 100+ Bgfx-registered pixel tests calls `GraphicsDevice::GetBackBufferData` at least once, most via the established `RunCheck()` pattern's own up-to-20-attempt outer retry loop (Task 406) layered on top of this inner 3-frame wait; the full `ctest` regression suite (4452 tests, including every Bgfx pixel test) has been run to completion at least 4 times in this session alone in this exact headless Xvfb/software-GL environment, with zero readback-timing-related failures ever observed (the only recurring failures — `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/`MipChain`, `RenderTargetCube_*` — are unrelated MSAA-resolve/depth-attachment driver limitations, not backbuffer-colour-readback timing). This constitutes real, extensive, repeated-in-practice reliability verification of the exact mechanism this row asks about; a synthetic dedicated test would need to sabotage the callback to ever exercise the timeout/throw branch at all (no legitimate scenario in this environment ever fails to fire within 3 frames), which wouldn't add confidence beyond what the existing suite's own scale already demonstrates. |

### SamplerState / TextureAddressMode

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |

| 743 | Audit `SamplerState` → `BGFX_SAMPLER_*` flag mapping completeness | ✅ | **2026-07-10 — real, confirmed, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed; closes the entire Phase 72 `SamplerState`/`TextureAddressMode` row group (743-749, all 7/7 done).** `BgfxGraphicsBackend::ApplySamplerState`'s filter `switch` only handled `Point`(1)/`Anisotropic`(2) explicitly — all 6 XNA `TextureFilter` values with a split Min/Mag/Mip combination (`LinearMipPoint`=3, `PointMipLinear`=4, `MinLinearMagPointMipLinear`=5, `MinLinearMagPointMipPoint`=6, `MinPointMagLinearMipLinear`=7, `MinPointMagLinearMipPoint`=8) silently fell through to the `default` (plain Linear, zero flags) branch, discarding the Min/Mag split entirely. Cross-referenced against `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::ApplySamplerState`, which already maps all 9 values correctly via GL's combined min-filter enum (`NearestMipmapLinear` etc.) — decoded each GL enum's Min/Mip components (real XNA semantics: the name encodes Min/Mag/Mip in that order) and translated to bgfx's 3 independent per-axis bits (`BGFX_SAMPLER_MIN_POINT`/`MAG_POINT`/`MIP_POINT`, actually a cleaner fit than GL's combined enum). Fixed by adding the 6 missing `case` labels. New `Bgfx_TextureFilter_SplitMinMag` test (`bgfx_texturefilter_split_minmag_test.cpp`) verifies the Mag-filter half of all 6 previously-broken values via magnification sampling (the Mip half can't be independently observed without a real generated mip chain, which CNA doesn't create by default) — every one of the 6 differs from at least one neighbor in its Mag component, so this fully discriminates "the split is applied" from "everything collapses to Linear." All 6/6 pass. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverted, reran — exactly the 3 `Mag=Point`-expecting checks failed as predicted (all read the coincidentally-still-plausible blended value instead), the 3 `Mag=Linear`-expecting checks coincidentally still passed (matches this session's now-familiar pattern of a wrong-default coincidentally agreeing with one specific real value); restored and reconfirmed 6/6 clean. Full Bgfx ctest regression (4456 tests, shared sampler-state code path used by every textured draw): zero new failures — the same 6 pre-existing `RenderTarget*` failures as every regression since Task 812/813. |

Confirms requesting an absurdly over-cap `MaxAnisotropy` (9999, far beyond any real GPU's limit) does not crash or throw on Bgfx — the literal, primary ask of this row (a true visual anisotropic-quality comparison is inherently driver-dependent and fragile to assert precisely, matching Task 299's own established reasoning). PASS, no crash. |
| 750 | Wire a Task-269-equivalent `SpriteBatch` `SamplerState` fix into Bgfx's `ISpriteBatchBackend` | ✅ | **Real bug confirmed and fixed — identical shape to Task 269 (EasyGL) and Task 665 (Vulkan).** `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend` never overrode `SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode` at all (silent no-op via `ISpriteBatchBackend`'s default empty bodies), so `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s `SamplerState` had zero effect — every draw used whatever `samplerFlags_[0]` happened to already hold from a previous 3D draw (or the zero-initialized default), never the sampler actually requested. Unlike EasyGL/Vulkan's deferred-batch architecture, Bgfx's `SpriteBatchBackend::Draw()` submits immediately per call (no separate flush step) via `BgfxGraphicsBackend::SubmitSprite()`, which already read `samplerFlags_[0]` via `bgfx::setTexture(0, textureSampler, textureHandle, samplerFlags_[0])` — the per-slot sampler-flags plumbing (`ApplySamplerState()`, already used by the 3D draw paths) was already fully in place; it just was never invoked from the sprite path. Fixed by adding `SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode` overrides storing `pendingFilter_`/`pendingAddressU_`/`pendingAddressV_` (mirroring EasyGL's exact field names/defaults — `TextureFilter::Linear`/`TextureAddressMode::Clamp`, `SpriteBatch`'s own real default), and calling `graphicsBackend.ApplySamplerState(0, pendingFilter_, pendingAddressU_, pendingAddressV_, 1)` immediately before each `SubmitSprite()` call. **New test** `examples/bgfx_texture_address_mode_test.cpp` — a direct port of Task 269/665's identical 2×1 Red/Blue-texture, `sourceRectangle` 2× width, `U≈1.25`-readback, `PointWrap`-vs-`PointClamp` methodology (retrying the backbuffer read per this project's established Bgfx first-read-per-frame convention): both checks pass with the exact predicted colors on the first attempt. **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted both changed files and rebuilt — the pre-fix code produced the exact predicted symptom, both `PointWrap` and `PointClamp` reading the **identical** wrong value `(254,0,1)` (proving `Wrap`/`Clamp` addressing genuinely never took effect either way, matching Task 665's exact pre-fix Vulkan symptom shape); restored and reconfirmed both checks pass. Full regression: `CnaTests` (Bgfx) 4276/4278 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); `ctest -R Bgfx` 52/54 (2 failures are the already-documented pre-existing `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` Xvfb limitation and `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain`, reconfirmed genuinely flaky in this sandbox via 3 direct isolated reruns — 2/3 passed, unrelated to this fix). |

This is the **exact same phenomenon Task 870 already found and fixed for Vulkan** (see `VulkanGraphicsBackend.cpp`'s own `PackDepthStencilBits`/`ds.front`/`ds.back` swap and its detailed comment) — previously undetected on Bgfx purely because no differential two-sided-stencil pixel test existed for it until this task. **Fixed** by swapping which XNA property set (`stencilFunc`/`stencilPass`/etc. vs `ccwStencilFunc`/`ccwStencilPass`/etc.) feeds `stencilFront_` vs `stencilBack_` in `ApplyDepthStencilState`, but only when `twoSidedStencilMode` is true (the single-sided fallback path, `stencilBack_ = stencilFront_`, is unaffected and unchanged). **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced the exact predicted failure (1/2, the two-sided check failing); restored and reconfirmed both checks pass. Full Bgfx regression: `ctest` 4426/4428 passed, 2 pre-existing (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/DRI3 limitation, `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain`/~1-in-8 timing flake), zero new. Cross-backend compile check: EasyGL/Vulkan/SDL_Renderer untouched (Bgfx-only fix). |

New `Bgfx_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil` test (a Bgfx-specific adaptation of `examples/easygl_graphicsdevice_reference_stencil_test.cpp`, Task 319 — that file reads back 2 regions in one frame, incompatible with Bgfx's `GetBackBufferData` "first read per frame" quirk, Task 406; restructured into one separately-read `RunCheck()` pass per check, mirroring Tasks 759-763's established Bgfx pattern): Check A (baseline, baked-in `ReferenceStencil=0x05` matching a stencil=0x05 stamp) expects PASS; Check B (same `DepthStencilState`, but `setReferenceStencilProperty(0x99)` called standalone afterward) expects the stencil test to now REJECT (0x99≠0x05) if the standalone override genuinely reaches the draw. **First run failed exactly as a real bug would**: Check B incorrectly stayed GREEN (the stale baked-in 0x05 was still used). **Fixed** by adding a real `BgfxGraphicsBackend::SetReferenceStencil` override: `ApplyDepthStencilState` now caches every stencil parameter except the reference value itself (`stencilEnableCached_`/`stencilFuncCached_`/etc., new private members) and delegates to a new `RebuildStencilState()` helper that reconstructs `stencilFront_`/`stencilBack_` via `BuildBgfxStencil`; `SetReferenceStencil(value)` updates only the cached reference and calls the same helper, letting a standalone reference change take effect without needing a full state re-application (mirrors Vulkan's `referenceStencil_` member/`Pending3DDraw::referenceStencil` pattern, Task 870/319, adapted for Bgfx's immediate — not deferred — draw submission). **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced the exact predicted failure (Check B incorrectly GREEN); restored and reconfirmed both checks pass. Full Bgfx regression: `ctest` 4427/4429 passed, 2 pre-existing (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/DRI3 limitation, `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain`/~1-in-8 timing flake), zero new. | 766 | Verify `FillMode::WireFrame` on Bgfx | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, confirmed, previously-undiscovered feature GAP found and fixed (a genuine implementation, not just a mapping-table bug — bgfx has no native polygon-fill-mode toggle at all, unlike D3D9/Vulkan's `VK_POLYGON_MODE_LINE`).** Confirmed via source: `BgfxGraphicsBackend::ApplyRasterizerState`'s `fillMode` parameter was entirely unused (commented out), and bgfx's own state flags/vendored `renderer_gl.cpp` have no line-primitive-substitution mechanism built in — this row's own hinted answer ("line-primitive substitution") is exactly what had to be built from scratch, mirroring `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::DrawWireframe`'s already-shipped emulation (re-expand each triangle's 3 vertices into a 6-index line-list edge buffer; no dedicated pixel test existed for this anywhere in the project prior to this task, on EasyGL or otherwise — confirmed via search). **Implementation**: (1) `BgfxIndexBufferBackend` gained a `cpuData` CPU-side byte cache (bgfx's `DynamicIndexBufferHandle` has no read-back API, mirroring `BgfxVertexBufferBackend::cpuData`'s existing precedent), populated in `SetData16`/`SetData32`; (2) new `wireframe_` member cached from `fillMode==1` in `ApplyRasterizerState`; (3) new `BgfxGraphicsBackend::ExpandWireframeIndices()` helper (mirrors `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::DrawWireframe`'s exact edge-expansion logic for `TriangleList`/`TriangleStrip`, synthesizing sequential indices when non-indexed) allocates a `bgfx::TransientIndexBuffer` and fills it with the expanded edges; (4) wired into all 4 Bgfx 3D draw-dispatch functions (`DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`/`DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx`) — when `wireframe_` is set and the primitive is a triangle type, the expanded transient buffer + `BGFX_STATE_PT_LINES` topology replace the normal vertex/index/topology submission; falls through to the existing solid path otherwise (zero behavior change for non-wireframe draws). New `Bgfx_RasterizerState_FillMode_WireFrame` test (adapted from `vulkan_fill_mode_test.cpp`, Task 327, restructured into per-check `RunCheck()` passes for Bgfx's `GetBackBufferData` "first read per frame" quirk, Task 406): Solid baseline (centre RED, interior filled) → WireFrame (centre BACKGROUND, interior NOT rasterized) → Solid reset (centre RED again, confirms the property genuinely reverts) — all 3/3 pass. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced the exact predicted failure (WireFrame check incorrectly showed RED, i.e. still solid-filled); restored and reconfirmed all 3 pass. Full Bgfx regression: `ctest` 4430/4431 passed, 1 pre-existing (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/DRI3 limitation), zero new — notably including every existing `SpriteBatch`/`Model`/`BasicEffect`/`SkinnedEffect`/`EnvironmentMapEffect`/`DualTextureEffect` test that exercises the 4 modified draw-dispatch functions, confirming the solid-fill path is unaffected. **2 adjacent, out-of-scope discoveries noted, not fixed here**: `BgfxGraphicsBackend` never overrides `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` (silently falls back to the base interface's `DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`, losing all `GpuDrawParams`-driven shader features — textures/lighting/fog/etc. — for any indexed+`GpuDrawParams` draw); and `DrawPrimitivesEx` never honors `params.vertexStart` in its existing solid-fill path (a pre-existing, separate bug — this task's own wireframe path threads it through faithfully per `vulkan_fill_mode_test.cpp`'s own reference behavior, so a non-zero `vertexStart` combined with `FillMode::WireFrame` would render more correctly than the same draw's solid path, an existing inconsistency this task did not introduce). Both are real, substantial, separately-scoped gaps worth their own future tasks. |
| 767 | Verify depth bias and slope-scale depth bias on Bgfx | ⬜ | **NEEDS_HUMAN (2026-07-10, Phase 72 gap-closure pass)** — confirmed via source, not just this row's own untested assumption: `BgfxGraphicsBackend::ApplyRasterizerState`'s `depthBias`/`slopeScaleDepthBias` parameters are both entirely unused (commented out), and bgfx's own high-level state API has **zero** depth-bias/polygon-offset mechanism anywhere — grepped `bgfx/defines.h` (no `BIAS`/`OFFSET` flag exists) and bgfx's own vendored `renderer_gl.cpp` (no `glPolygonOffset` call anywhere in the whole file). This is a permanent bgfx high-level API ceiling, not a narrow mapping bug like Tasks 758-766's findings. **EasyGL has the exact same gap already** (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend::ApplyRasterizerState`'s own `depthBias`/`slopeScaleDepthBias` params are likewise both unused) — only Vulkan implements real depth bias (`vkCmdSetDepthBias`, dynamic state). A genuine fix would require emulating depth bias via a shader-level Z-offset (a uniform/push-constant added to every 3D vertex shader's output depth), and slope-scale bias specifically needs the polygon's screen-space depth *slope*, which only a fragment-stage `dFdx`/`dFdy` computation can approximate accurately — a cross-cutting architecture change spanning shader code on (at least) both EasyGL and Bgfx, not a small Bgfx-scoped fix like this row's neighbors. Needs a project-owner decision on whether/how to pursue shader-level emulation (and whether to do EasyGL and Bgfx together, given they share the identical gap) before further work — deliberately not attempted here to avoid a rushed, half-considered shader change affecting every 3D draw path. |
| 768 | Verify scissor test enable/disable interaction with `ScissorRectangle` on Bgfx | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2 real, confirmed, previously-undiscovered bugs found and fixed.** New `Bgfx_Scissor` test (a Bgfx-specific adaptation of `examples/easygl_scissor_test.cpp`, Task 209 — that file draws full-screen 3 times and reads 2 spatially-separate columns per draw in the SAME frame, incompatible with Bgfx's `GetBackBufferData` "first read per frame" quirk, Task 406; restructured into one separately-read `RunCheck()` pass per (`RasterizerState`, column) pair, mirroring Tasks 759-767's established Bgfx pattern). **Bug 1 (the larger one, found first)**: `bgfx::setScissor` was called in exactly ONE place in the entire backend — the 2D `SpriteBatch` draw path — and NONE of the 4 3D draw-dispatch functions (`DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`/`DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx`) ever called it; `RasterizerState.ScissorTestEnable`/`GraphicsDevice.ScissorRectangle` had zero effect on any 3D draw. Fixed with a new shared `ApplyScissorOverride()` helper (bgfx resets scissor per-draw-call, so it must be called before every submit, confirmed against bgfx's own `bgfx::RenderDraw::m_scissor` semantics) wired into all 4 functions. **Bug 2 (found while verifying Bug 1's own fix)**: `scissorW_`/`scissorH_` used a "zero means disabled" sentinel conflating two independent pieces of XNA state — `RasterizerState.ScissorTestEnable` (set via `ApplyRasterizerState`) and `GraphicsDevice.ScissorRectangle`'s own coordinates (set via a separate `SetScissorRect` call, in either order) — so calling `SetScissorRect` with a real rect AFTER disabling the test silently re-enabled clipping. Confirmed EasyGL avoids this entirely via 2 genuinely independent GL calls (`set_scissor_test_enabled`/`set_scissor`). Fixed with a new, real `scissorEnabled_` boolean member, set only by `ApplyRasterizerState`, checked independently of the rect's own dimensions in `ApplyScissorOverride()`. **First run (with only Bug 1 fixed) failed exactly as Bug 2 predicts**: 5/7 checks passed, both `ScissorOff` checks with a right-half rect still SET (from a prior `SetScissorRect` call) incorrectly showed clipping. All 7/7 pass after both fixes (6 pixel checks + 1 getter/setter round-trip, which needed no Bgfx-specific restructuring). **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild** (both fixes stashed together): reverting reproduced the original Bug 1 failure pattern exactly (6/7, only the "ScissorOn" clipping check failing, confirming scissor genuinely has zero effect on 3D pre-fix); restored and reconfirmed 7/7. Full Bgfx regression: `ctest` 4431/4432 passed, 1 pre-existing (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/DRI3 limitation), zero new — notably including every existing `SpriteBatch` test exercising the shared 2D scissor code path this task also touched (replaced its inline duplicate check with the same `ApplyScissorOverride()` helper). |

| 773 | Verify `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)` restores the backbuffer on Bgfx | ✅ | **2026-07-10 — no bug found, real coverage gap closed, superseding a stale claim.** New `examples/bgfx_setrendertarget_null_restore_test.cpp`. Task 179's own original smoke test (`bgfx_render_target_usage_test.cpp`) carried a stale header comment ("Pixel-level verification is not available in the Bgfx backend") predating both Task 406's now-pervasive `GetBackBufferData` convention and Task 901's `EnsureViewState()` fix (which specifically corrected a bug where a bound `RenderTarget2D`'s smaller viewport/ortho-projection leaked into subsequent backbuffer draws) — this task supersedes that stale claim with real assertions. Binds a 16×16 RT, clears it Red, calls `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)`, clears the real 64×64 backbuffer Green, draws a full-viewport Blue quad via `SpriteBatch`, and reads back both the centre AND a corner outside the RT's own smaller bounds — both correctly read Blue, confirming the backbuffer is genuinely restored at its own full size, not clipped/misaligned to the RT's dimensions. **Found and fixed a real, adjacent test-methodology bug of my own along the way** (not a production bug): an early draft repeatedly rebound the SAME `RenderTarget2D` object across many separate rendered frames inside the retry loop, which triggered a crash — the same pre-existing `glReadPixels`/`GL_INVALID_OPERATION` class of failure as the already-documented `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_DepthBuffer`/`MsaaResolve` baseline (Task 812/813's own finding) — fixed by binding the RT only once (a `rtBoundOnce_` guard), not once per retry attempt; this is a real, previously-undiscovered environmental trigger for that known limitation, documented here rather than chased further, matching established precedent. **Discriminating-power investigation, transparently documented rather than skipped**: attempted sabotage of both `SetRenderTarget2D(nullptr)`'s `spriteViewId`/`currentViewId_` reset and its `currentRtWidth_`/`currentRtHeight_` reset — neither broke the test even after direct debug instrumentation of `EnsureViewState()` confirmed the sabotaged state genuinely persisted. Root cause: bgfx resets a view's frame-buffer binding back to the default swapchain every new frame unless explicitly re-set that same frame, so a leftover stale view/FBO binding only ever manifests within the SAME frame as the bind — the multi-frame `RunCheck()` retry loop this project's own Task 406 convention requires cannot keep that stale state observable long enough to sabotage-and-refail. Documented transparently in the test's own file header rather than silently omitted, matching this project's established precedent for investigated-but-inherently-non-discriminating scenarios (Task 923's alpha-blend half). Full Bgfx ctest regression (4457 tests): zero new failures — the same 6 pre-existing `RenderTarget*` failures as every regression since Task 812/813. |
| 774 | Verify MRT with mixed formats is rejected or handled per XNA constraints on Bgfx | ✅ | **2026-07-10 — real, confirmed, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed, in SHARED code affecting all 4 backends.** Research first: read FNA's own real `GraphicsDevice.SetRenderTargets` (`FNA/src/Graphics/GraphicsDevice.cs`) — it performs ZERO surface-format validation at all, forwarding every `RenderTargetBinding` straight to FNA3D with no format-compatibility check whatsoever. The real, already-shipped constraint lives one level earlier: `Texture::ValidateFormat` (Task 176's contract), called by every format-taking `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` constructor, throws for every `SurfaceFormat` except `Color` on all 4 backends — meaning mixed-format MRT should be provably unreachable (no two differently-formatted render targets could ever be constructed to bind together). **Verifying this directly for `RenderTarget2D` specifically (not just the already-tested plain `Texture2D` case) found the real gap**: `RenderTarget2D`'s constructor delegates to a separate, dedicated `Texture2D(device, w, h, fmt, levelCount, backend)` overload ("used exclusively by RenderTarget2D," per its own doc comment) that **skipped `ValidateFormat` entirely** — `RenderTarget2D(dev, 4, 4, false, SurfaceFormat::Bgr565, DepthFormat::None)` silently succeeded instead of throwing, even though `CreateRenderTarget2D`'s own backend call never actually forwards the requested format (always allocates a real Color-format GPU resource regardless) — meaning a `RenderTarget2D` could report a non-Color `Format()` while its real backing texture was silently always Color. **The identical gap existed in `TextureCube`'s own analogous delegating constructor** (used exclusively by `RenderTargetCube`) — found via the same investigation before assuming it was Texture2D-only. **Fixed both**: added `ValidateFormat(fmt)`/`Texture::ValidateFormat(format)` to the start of each constructor body, matching the exact pattern their own non-RT sibling constructors already use. New `examples/bgfx_mrt_mixed_formats_test.cpp` (2 checks: 2 same-format `Color` RTs bind together fine; a `Bgr565` `RenderTarget2D` now correctly throws at construction, before `SetRenderTargets` is ever reached) plus a verbatim reuse of Task 176's own `easygl_surface_format_throws_test.cpp` (100% backend-agnostic, pure CPU-side construction) registered for Bgfx for the first time, confirming the same Color-only contract holds there too (16/16 pass). **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting both production files reproduced the exact predicted failure (mixed-format check FAILED, 1/2); restored and reconfirmed 2/2 clean. **Full regression on all 4 backends, since this is shared code** (`Texture2D.cpp`/`TextureCube.cpp`): Bgfx `ctest` 4459 tests, zero new failures (same 6 pre-existing `RenderTarget*` failures); EasyGL `ctest` 4539 tests, zero new failures (3 known baseline + 1 reconfirmed-in-isolation unrelated Audio flake, `DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.BufferNeededFiresExactlyTheStarvedCount`); Vulkan `ctest` 4455 tests, zero new failures (exactly the 4 known baseline: 3 `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` segfaults + `Vulkan_DepthBias`); SDL_Renderer `ctest` 4428 tests, zero new failures (exactly the 11 known baseline). |
| 775 | Document Bgfx MRT attachment limits | ✅ | **2026-07-10 — pure documentation, closes the entire Phase 72 `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` row group (773-775, all 3/3 done).** New "Bgfx MRT attachment limits" subsection in `docs/graphics-backend-feature-matrix.md`: the practical, enforced limit is FNA's own real `MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS = 4` (Task 881's shared-code gate, rejects anything larger before any backend sees the call); `BgfxGraphicsBackend::SetRenderTargets`'s own local `kMaxAttachments = 8` (matching bgfx's `BGFX_CONFIG_MAX_FRAME_BUFFER_ATTACHMENTS` default) is unreachable in practice, kept as defense-in-depth; the real device capability (`bgfx::getCaps()->limits.maxFBAttachments`, logged at startup per Task 456) is typically 8 on this project's desktop hardware, always ≥ the FNA-mandated 4, so it has never been the binding constraint. |

### SpriteBatch (needs Task 740's readback wiring first)

| 803 | Pixel test: `SpriteSortMode` ordering (Deferred/Texture/FrontToBack/BackToFront) on Bgfx | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, confirmed, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed, in SHARED code affecting all 4 backends, not just Bgfx.** New `Bgfx_SpriteBatch_LayerDepthOrder` test — a Bgfx-specific adaptation of `examples/easygl_spritebatch_layerdepth_test.cpp` (Task 420 — that file reads back 3 spatially-separate regions from ONE rendered frame, incompatible with Bgfx's `GetBackBufferData` "first read per frame" quirk, Task 406; restructured into one separately-read `RunCheck()` pass per region, mirroring Tasks 759-768's established Bgfx pattern), matching Task 420's own established scope note: Tasks 415/416 already prove the CPU-side `SpriteSortMode` sort logic itself is correct and backend-agnostic (mock-backend tests, all 4 modes); this task (like Task 420 before it) verifies the remaining backend-specific question — that whatever order the backend receives sorted draws in is actually reflected on screen via painter's algorithm, using `FrontToBack` as the representative mode. **First run failed exactly as a real bug would**: the overlap region showed the WRONG sprite on top (Red instead of the expected Blue), even though `SpriteBatch::flushBatch()`'s CPU-side sort correctly reordered the draws before dispatch. **Root-caused to `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s final overload never applying its `depthStencilState` parameter at all** (`DepthStencilState* /*depthStencilState*/` — silently discarded, matching this project's now-familiar "commented-out unused parameter" bug shape) — SpriteBatch draws therefore inherited whatever `DepthStencilState` a game's own 3D rendering last configured (or each backend's own construction-time default) instead of FNA's real default of `DepthStencilState.None` when the caller passes null. **This is genuinely SHARED code** (`src/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/SpriteBatch.cpp`), not Bgfx-specific — it was masked on EasyGL/Vulkan purely by coincidence: OpenGL's own true hardware default has depth testing DISABLED (never explicitly enabled by Task 420's own test, which never calls any DepthStencilState-setting method), so simple painter's algorithm worked there by accident, not because SpriteBatch correctly managed the state. Bgfx's own `BgfxGraphicsBackend::depthFlags_` class member defaults to a NON-trivial, depth-test-ENABLED value (`BGFX_STATE_DEPTH_TEST_LESS \| BGFX_STATE_WRITE_Z`), which doesn't coincidentally match, exposing the real, pre-existing gap for the first time. With depth test active and all sprite vertices at the same implicit Z, `DEPTH_TEST_LESS` rejects the second-drawn (later) sprite's fragments at any overlap, always leaving whichever sprite was drawn FIRST on top — regardless of the CPU-side sort's own correct decision about which sprite should visually win. **Fixed** by applying `depthStencilState ? *depthStencilState : DepthStencilState::None` via `graphicsDevice_->setDepthStencilStateProperty(...)` in `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s final overload, mirroring the already-correct `samplerState` handling immediately below it (null always means the FNA-documented default, and the state is always re-applied, never left over from a previous `Begin()`). A speculative first attempt at fixing this via `bgfx::setViewMode(spriteViewId, bgfx::ViewMode::Sequential)` (bgfx's own default `ViewMode::Default` sorts by internal state/program key, not submission order) turned out to be unnecessary once the real depth-state bug was fixed — tested and removed to keep the fix minimal, not left in as unnecessary defensive code. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced the exact predicted failure; restored and reconfirmed all 3 checks pass. **Full regression run on all 4 backends, since this is shared code**: Bgfx `ctest` 4435/4437 passed (2 pre-existing: `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/DRI3 limitation, 1 unrelated Audio flake reconfirmed via isolation re-run); EasyGL 4536/4539 passed (3 pre-existing: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`/Task 145, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`/Task 872, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`/sibling-repo); Vulkan 4451/4455 passed (4 pre-existing: 3 `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` segfaults + `Vulkan_DepthBias`); SDL_Renderer 4417/4428 passed (11 pre-existing, all confirmed via git-stash-revert re-run to fail identically without this fix too — SDL_Renderer's own documented 2D-only-backend limitations, e.g. `CreateIndexBuffer16` throws). **Zero new failures on any of the 4 backends.** This is arguably the most impactful single finding of this session's entire Phase 72 pass, since it affects every game's SpriteBatch rendering on every backend whenever depth state happens to be non-default when SpriteBatch.Begin() is called (e.g., drawing a HUD/UI overlay via SpriteBatch immediately after 3D depth-tested content). |

All 3/3 pass on the first attempt (marker rotated to the exact FNA-formula-predicted screen position, sprite interior correctly filled away from the marker, no stray coverage outside the sprite), confirming `BgfxGraphicsBackend`'s sprite rotation math (`rotateAndTranslate` in the sprite `Draw` path) is already correct. **Discriminating power verified via sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily swapped signs in the rotation matrix (`+y*sinR`/`-y*cosR` instead of `-y*sinR`/`+y*cosR`) — 2 of 3 checks failed as predicted (marker no longer at the rotated position, sprite interior no longer covering its expected point), reverted and reconfirmed 3/3. | 808 | Pixel test: `transformMatrix` in `SpriteBatch::Begin` on Bgfx | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, confirmed, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed; closes the entire Phase 72 `SpriteBatch` row group (803-808).** New `Bgfx_SpriteBatch_TransformMatrix` test — a Bgfx-specific adaptation of `examples/easygl_transform_matrix_test.cpp` (Task 168, already reused verbatim on Vulkan): that file reads back 2 spatially-separate regions from one rendered frame, incompatible with Bgfx's `GetBackBufferData` "first read per frame" quirk (Task 406); restructured into one separately-read `RunCheck()` pass per region, mirroring Tasks 759-807's established Bgfx pattern. **First run failed exactly as a real bug would**: a 1x1 red sprite drawn at (0,0) under a `Matrix::CreateTranslation(100,50,0)` `transformMatrix` never actually moved — `ISpriteBatchBackend::SetTransformMatrix`'s base interface default is a no-op (`{}`), and `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend` never overrode it (same "silent no-op via default empty body" shape Task 750 already fixed for `SpriteBatch::Begin`'s `SamplerState` parameter on this exact class). **Fixed** by: (1) overriding `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::SetTransformMatrix` to store the matrix on a new `BgfxGraphicsBackend::spriteTransform_` member (defaults to identity, matching every `SpriteBatch::Begin()` overload's own null default); (2) folding it into `EnsureViewState()`'s existing ortho-projection computation, combined via `bx::mtxMul` before `bgfx::setViewTransform` — safe for 3D draws sharing the same view id, since 3D shaders supply their own per-draw world-view-projection uniform and never read bgfx's built-in view/proj that `setViewTransform` populates. **A genuine matrix-multiplication-order bug was found and fixed while implementing this**: the first attempt (`bx::mtxMul(result, ortho, transform)`) left the origin check passing but the translated-position check failing — root-caused to bx's own vector convention being ROW-vector (`bx::vec4MulMtx`'s own doc comment: "row vector _vec by matrix _mat", i.e. `v' = v * M`), meaning the combined matrix for "apply transform first, then project" needed the OPPOSITE argument order (`bx::mtxMul(result, transform, ortho)`), matching EasyGL's own `transform_ * orthoM` semantics exactly once corrected. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced the exact original failure (both checks failing); restored and reconfirmed both checks pass. Full Bgfx regression: `ctest` 4441/4442 passed, 1 pre-existing (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/DRI3 limitation), zero new — notably including every existing 3D rendering test (`BasicEffect`/`SkinnedEffect`/`EnvironmentMapEffect`/`DualTextureEffect`/etc.), confirming the shared `EnsureViewState()` change has zero effect on 3D draws as predicted. |

## Phase 74 — XNA compiled effect bytecode (`.fx`) support via MojoShader

Opened by Task 352's full-support decision. Full research/reasoning behind this phase lives in
`docs/fx-bytecode-support-plan.md` — read that first. Dedicated task-number block (`10200`+),
mirroring the WebGPU backend's `10000`+ convention (Phases 56–69), since this is comparable in
scope: a new vendored native dependency, two independent per-backend GPU-shader-translation
paths, an unknown-feasibility third backend, and real test-fixture sourcing with no XNA Content
Pipeline tooling available in this project.
